## Goal

Decompile Lemmings for Windows 95 (`WINLEMM.zip`, original files in `orig/WINLEMM/`: LEMMINGS.EXE + VSMEM, READDIB, FILEHAND, GAMEOVER, LOADLVL, RENDER, PROCESS DLLs) into source that builds two ways from the same files:

1. **Matching:** byte-for-byte with the original under Visual C++ 2.2 (`build/vc22`, `/Ox`, run under Wine by `tools/msvc/cl.sh`).
2. **Portable:** llvm-mingw (`make`) with our own small CRT (`src/crt`), verified by running the game in Wine and comparing logs with the original.

Read `docs/DECOMP.md` first: it is the method document (layout, the matching loop, and what is known about the compiler and the author's habits).

Backup `build/scratch/gameview.cpp.s29_before`.
- **ApplyOptions: a real ordering bug fixed (22:00):** the original stores `g_resolutionNew = size;` **before** `SetZoom(...)` in the "restart this level" branch; ours stored it after, so `SetZoom` (which sizes the window from `g_scale * g_resolutionNew`, frame.cpp) used the *old* resolution when switching resolution during a game. Fixed in `src/lemmings/options.cpp` (backup `build/scratch/options.cpp.s29_before`). The reorder shifted the registers (22 insns); `int size = 0;` initialised at its declaration + 11 `pad_applyoptions` lines brings it to **7** (was 2 with the wrong order). The remaining difference is only where `size = 0` executes (declaration-init hoists the `xor edi,edi` to the top; the original does it later) — find a form that keeps registers but sinks that store. options.cpp 2/3, EXE 313/334 unchanged (`build/scratch/s29_exe_after_options.log`), `make` OK, **Wine HD level 1 (`s29-l1`) and digger (`s29-dig`) MATCH** (also covers the ScrollTo change); the in-game resolution switch itself is not covered by any Wine scenario. **Lesson: a "same shape, 2 instruction" miss can be a statement-order bug; compare store positions, not only registers.**

**Verification recipe for any change (do all, in this order, nothing else compiling):**
1. Whole file: `tools/msvc/try.sh MODULE src/.../file.cpp` must not lose any previous match; if it does, re-pad: `python3 tools/msvc/padplan.py MODULE FILE --max 64 --jobs 8 --start 0xADDR_OF_FUNCTION_BEFORE_FIRST_BROKEN --write`.
2. Whole module: `python3 tools/msvc/build.py --funcs LEMMINGS > LOG` (or PROCESS/RENDER); diff its `: DIFF` list against the previous log (`build/scratch/gv/funcs2.log` for the EXE) — only your function may change. `python3 tools/msvc/mapcmp.py LEMMINGS --sec .bss` must say 0 wrong.
3. `make`, then `SILENT=1 MODS="VSMEM READDIB FILEHAND GAMEOVER LOADLVL RENDER PROCESS LEMMINGS" tools/wine/run.sh NAME 300 ''` vs `build/runs/hd-base-l1.txt` and `... 400 'waitupd 200;click 332 395;waitupd 228;click 596 138'` vs `hd-base-dig.txt` (`python3 tools/cmplog.py BASE NEW`). `SILENT=1` = no sound, minimized window. Never run Wine while compile searches run.
4. Regenerate the progress table: `python3 build/scratch/progress_table.py RENDER=build/scratch/s40_restore/render_full.log PROCESS=build/scratch/s48_sound_wave/process_full.log LEMMINGS=<new log>` and paste into the table at the top of Current State.

**Search tools (all in `tools/msvc/`, all score with `shapes.py`; results/specs land in `build/scratch/<tool>/<addr>/`):** `shapes.py MODULE SOURCE ADDR SPEC.py` (SPEC.py: `SLOTS=[(orig_body, [alt_body,...])]`), `lifetimes.py` (intermediate copies / hoisting), `typeshuffle.py` (signedness + declaration order), `fuzz.py` (random combos incl. init-at-declaration, dummy locals), `cacheglobals.py` (cache read-only globals in locals), `mutate.py` (micro-rewrites; **its `a+b` swap is not precedence-safe, verify hits semantically**), `dummies`-style scripts in `build/scratch/gv/`. A DIFF line without "N insns differ" is a relocation-only difference (code identical; usually a wrong name in the names file) and scores 9999 — look at it, don't discard it.

**The lab technique that found TreeEnsureVisible (use it first on a near-miss):** write a tiny standalone `.c/.cpp` replica in `build/scratch/gv/lab/`, compile with `tools/msvc/cl.sh /nologo /c /Ox /FoX.obj X.cpp`, disassemble with `i686-w64-mingw32-objdump -d -M intel X.obj`, vary the source form, and find which spelling flips the instruction/operand/register choice; then transplant. Facts learned: VC always narrows a product feeding a `short` to 16-bit; `cmp` operand order flips between a 16-bit *local* and an inline *expression* operand; any unused extra local can reshuffle register allocation (IconWnd::WndProc 115→44) so the real cause is often a missing *used* variable; the declaration *types and order* of unrelated locals decide tie-breaks (CreateFrontDib); local function pointers pin which import pointers get cached (WinMain, ApplyOptions 23→13).

**Open misses, best state, and what is already ruled out (don't repeat):**
- EXE small: ApplyOptions 2 (cmp operand order `cmp [g_zoom],esi` + `g_resolutionNew = size` before SetZoom; store-first body is 8 with `int size = 0;` at the top, 13 with a local IsDlgButtonChecked pointer), DockWnd::DockWnd 4 (original pushes/pops an unused `edi`, stores bkColor before hidden), BuildRows 4 (ours hoists `mov edi,0`, original `xor edi,edi` in the else block), MapView::DrawLemmings 7 (counter/pointer register swap), OnVScroll 11 with ternary clamp (original `cur`=eax,`n`=ecx; ours reversed; clamp file `build/scratch/gv/gameview.vs2.cpp` — applying it needs padplan), ReadIniSettings 10, CloseLevelDialog 12, InitStatBar 10-15, ScrollTo 20-22 (original does a 32-bit multiply then 16-bit dec/not for the mask; VC always narrows to 16-bit in every form tried), OnStartLevel DONE.
- EXE large: SetViewScale 46, IconWnd::WndProc 115 (any extra local → 44, residual is a repeated counter/pointer register swap across its 13-loops), StartGame 50, SaveLayout 29, LayoutWindows 21, DrawPane 31, LevelDlgProc 29, OpenLevelItem 34, DrawPreview 36, ShowLevelDetails 37, DockWnd::Drag 29, Button::DrawFace 29.
- PROCESS (9): SoundInit 1 (original preheader `xor edx; xor ecx; mov eax` vs ours `xor ecx; mov eax; xor edx`; lab shows ours for ~10 spellings), Builder::Process 3 (second GetPixel call's `lea ecx` temp + `mov ecx,g_map` after both pushes), FillObjects 5 (original compares loaded `rows`/`cols` temps FIRST; assigning them to locals before the condition flips the order but changes load placement), WaveTestVolume 13, AddTrapType 17, AddObjectType 21, Miner 25, Basher 27, BuildCollision 32.
- RENDER (4): StretchChanged 3, Renderer_Render 7, MakeDIBSection 10 (original loads constant 1 once into eax; compiler folds it back to immediates in every form), CopyChanged 17 (original has StretchChanged's structure; rewrite gives same shape but 24+).
- Ruled out broadly (hundreds of builds each): statement permutations, chains, counter names/types, goto/switch layouts, inline helpers, mid-block declarations, parameter-type variants, random declaration orders, dummy locals, cacheglobals/fuzz/mutate batches over every EXE miss (`build/scratch/gv/batch_*.log`).

**Suggested next steps (highest odds first):** (1) apply the lab technique to Builder::Process, FillObjects, SoundInit and DrawLemmings with replicas closer to the real function (include the real class types); (2) try REA (`mcp__rea__*`, provider `hopper`; close with `close_binary` or Hopper wedges; Hopper has no procedure for dialog-proc-style functions like ApplyOptions/OnVScroll) on StretchChanged/Renderer_Render to compare original structure; (3) the big misses only after the small ones; (4) keep `docs/DECOMP.md` current with each new compiler fact (the TreeEnsureVisible compare-order rule and the CreateFrontDib signedness trick are not yet in it).

**Cautions:** another agent has worked in this tree concurrently — check `ps aux | grep -E "shapes|fuzz|padplan|wine"` before starting searches and don't kill processes you didn't start. Adding `int step = (short)w;` brings back the original's header stores through eax, the 16-bit `x` load and the separate sign-extended step, but at 27 (fx in cx, step in di, counter edx); a 1,000-sample declaration × pad 0-63 search (`build/scratch/s24_intx/aot_step_decl.log`) stays 27. Same-body swap between the two functions: each still best with its own body (21/17).
  - Open question for a match: what source makes VC hoist `(int)(short)w` into a scratch register while keeping fx 16-bit. Candidate files: `aot_step.txt` (step local), `aot_types.txt`, `step.txt`.
- **Miner::Process 25 (session 24, scratch only, source unchanged):** besides the switch value's register (ecx ours / edx original) the **jump tables differ**. Original index table (frames 0-23): `[0,1,1,2,13,3,13,4,13,5,13,6,13,7,13,8,13,9,13,10,13,11,13,12]` — every odd case 5..23 except 15 has its own slot (all jumping to the end, 0x10003d8b), default = 13. Ours: `[0,1,1,2,8,8,8,3,8,8,8,4,8,8,8,5,8,8,8,6,8,8,8,7]` — VC folds cases 5, 9, 13, 17, 21 into the default slot and keeps only 7, 11, 19, 23 (+15). Identical table for: one grouped `case 5: … case 23: break;`, two groups, each case with its own `break`, each with `return`, a grouped `return`, an explicit `default: break;`, an empty statement, and 6 different case orders (`build/scratch/s24_intx/miner_cases.txt`, `miner_order.txt`, `miner_ret.txt`); removing the odd cases entirely gives 24 (table shrinks to 16 entries). Helper to read a compiled table: `python3 build/scratch/s24_intx/jt.py OBJ` (reads the byte table a `mov al,[reg+$L…]` points at). Open question: what source makes VC 2.2 keep a separate slot for every listed case — maybe the cases had code in the original that was optimised to nothing, or a different case set.
- **Switch-slot probe (session 24, user request):** `build/scratch/s24_intx/probe/probe*.cpp` + `jt2.py` (prints each function's index table and which slots share a target). Rule found (also in docs/DECOMP.md): a case group gets one slot per run of its values only if its code differs from the default's at table-build time; empty-`break`/`return`/`;`/`if (0)`/empty-inline/`x = x` groups are folded into default (our table). A dead block `{ int t = l->frame; }` or `if (l->frame & 1) break;` in the group reproduces the original's index table **exactly**, but VC then gives the group a private epilogue copy (the original has three epilogues: after case 0, after cases 1/2, and the shared end 0x10003d8b used by 3/15, the odd group and default), so Miner scores 39-46 (`miner_dead.txt`, `miner_dead2.txt`; without the trailing break: same). Source unchanged (25). Open: a group body that VC sees as code when building the table but that reaches the shared end without duplicating the epilogue.
- **Miner continued (session 24):** two outcomes only, never both at once. (a) Group bodies the front end drops (`;`, `l->frame;`, `(void)l;`, `x = x;`, `if (0)`, empty inline, `return`, `goto end`): our folded table + 3 epilogues = current 25. (b) Bodies that survive until the table is built (dead stores `y = l->y;`, `n = 0;`, `x = l->x;`, `at.x = 0;`, dead block `{ int t = l->frame; }`, `if (l->frame & 1) break;`): the original's table but a 4th epilogue (the group's `jmp end` gets tail-duplicated; VC lays the group block out inside case 3/15's code at ~+0xd6 whatever its source position — first, before case 3, after 1/2, last: all the same), 39-46. In the original the group's (empty) block evidently sits last, right before the shared end at 0x10003d8b, so its slots point at the end without a jump. Files: `miner_dead*.txt`, `miner_pos.txt`. Source unchanged (25).
- **AddTrapType 17 (session 24, no gain, source unchanged):** the 24 AddObjectType body shapes (`aot_types.txt` + `aot_step.txt`, i.e. int/short fx × int/short current × store orders, step locals) × pads 0-31 give 17 at best (`int fx; short current`, ties the current body); a 640-sample declaration × pad 0-63 search on the current body stays 17 (`build/scratch/s24_intx/trap_decl.log`). Its remaining difference is the same as AddObjectType's: the original's 16-bit `movsx eax,ax; add ax,cx` step with `(short)w` sign-extended once into ecx, plus the header-store scheduling. Both functions wait on the same open question (what source gives that step with fx kept 16-bit and `list` spilled).

Making the clamp live (`static int saved; saved = volume;` after the clamp, `wave2.txt` body 2) gives **11**, but that writes memory the original doesn't; the original's two callee-saved registers are **esi = clamped volume (kept across all three calls)** and **edi = the header pointer** (`mov edi,eax` after LoadWave), and it passes the string at 0x10011b0c straight to LoadWave. ## LEMMINGS.EXE (in progress)

### .bss hash CRACKED (session 34, 2026-10-25)
- Found in the compiler: C1XX.EXE's tokenizer (0x40b5e2) hashes identifiers with **h = 4*h + c + (h >> 7)** (32-bit, from 0; its own name table uses h & 0x7ff). **.bss order within an object = ascending `H(name) & 0x3ff`**, later-defined first within a bucket. Verified: 2,500 random names (0 inconsistent classes, 0 order violations) and 21/23 of our EXE objects + all PROCESS objects; the 2 exceptions are compiler-generated static-init guards (`_?$$1000@...`), which are not hashed (endscreen: first, frontend: last).
- Tool: `python3 tools/msvc/bsshash.py name...` (bucket) and `--check OBJ...`.
- Found by scanning C1XX/C2 disassembly (`i686-w64-mingw32-objdump -d -M intel`) for short NUL-terminated byte loops with shift/lea arithmetic; C2 only has the section names.
- **.bss layout DONE (session 34, cont.): `mapcmp.py LEMMINGS --sec .bss` → 0 symbols at the wrong address.** ~80 globals renamed to natural-looking names whose buckets ascend (planner `build/scratch/s34_bss/plan2.py`, plans plan2.txt/plan3.txt, rename lists renames.txt/ren3.txt), POINTs g_mapPos/g_iconPos split into ints (original keeps them 4-aligned), 6 `unused` fillers for gaps, 40 inferred addresses (endscreen/levelsel/splash/replay, via `tools/msvc/bssaddr.py` with MATCHED=) added to tools/names. Static-init guards sit at fixed keys (endscreen ≈85, frontend ≈764; `guardprobe.py`). Pre-rename source backup: `build/scratch/s34_bss/src.before/`.
- **After re-tune (session 34): full link 297/334, .bss still 0 wrong** (`build/scratch/s34_bss/funcs5.log`, tune log `tune.log`). Then `padplan --start 0x410620` on map.cpp restored MapView::WndProc → **298/334**. Still lost vs the 300 baseline: OnScrollTimer, SetViewCursor (gameview, operand/reloc order of g_zoom/g_resolutionNew and g_scrollSpeedOld), Button::Button (icons, 6 insns); gained GetFrameHeight. Hypothesis "commutative operand order = lower name bucket" tested and REJECTED (renaming g_resolutionNew to buckets 630/355 changed nothing). Running: revert-one-rename-at-a-time search on gameview (temp edits, auto-restored; pristine copy `$TMPDIR/srcbak_res`) to find which renamed symbol flips them.
- (history) Renames shifted register ties: full link **279/334** before re-tuning (`build/scratch/s34_bss/funcs4.log`). The session-34 re-tune log is complete at `build/scratch/s34_bss/tune.log`.

So a 4-aligned table in the original is a scalar + an array: split `g_unused2fc` + `g_unusedTable[23]` (mainwnd) and `g_unused744` + `g_unused748[6]` (frontend); names file updated.

A file-scope `char *const` does NOT fold (code loads the pointer), so that is not it. endscreen `g_unused4fc` + `g_unused500[6]` (same split as frontend), register.cpp starts with an unused `int g_unused45c = 0;`. - **.bss hash, partly cracked (parked; resume here for a byte-identical EXE):** within a file, uninitialised globals are emitted in ascending bucket key; names in one bucket come later-defined first. Probe tools (all in build/scratch): `bssprobe.py name...` (prints the .bss order of `int name;` lines), `bssclasses.py NAMES.txt OUT.json` (compiles forward and reversed: names whose order flips share a bucket), `bucket.py name...` (labels each name's bucket by the 3-char reference it ties with, label = h mod 1022). Facts, h = (h << 2) + c over the plain name:
  - 1–2-char names: key = h exactly (consistent with every observation).
  - 3-char names tie exactly when h ≡ h' (mod 1022) (969 names, no conflict).
  - 4-char names: label = h + 12 (near 'aaaa', h≈7200–8300) to h + 16 (h ≳ 8790), with a few ±1 runs; 5-char: about h + 108..112.
  - But 'bxxy' sits in a bucket strictly between 'Bwz' and 'Bxw' (3-char, consecutive h): so 3-char names do NOT cover every bucket, the table is larger than 1022 and the key is not a function of h alone (char-dependent weights?).
  - Ruled out: (h << k) ± or ^ c with k = 1..8 mod any M in 500..8192 (also 16-bit, reversed, decorated `?name@@3HA`), char transforms (upper/lower/&0x1f/^0x20), per-step mod/fold/rotate variants.
  - Next idea: fit K = Σ c_i·w_i(L) mod T with unknown weights per length from ordering data; or skip the hash and pick names by search with the compiler as oracle.

**Layout facts found:**
- LIBC starts at 0x411c50 (`??2@YAPAXI@Z` operator new is 0x411c50; `build/scratch/libcfind.py EXE LIBC.LIB` finds 80 LIBC objects). Game code = 0x401000–0x411c50, about 375 functions.
- **File boundaries:** functions padded with `int3` (not `lea`/`mov edi,edi`) are COMDATs = inline member functions, emitted right after their object's own code (after the `_$$2000` thunk too). List in `build/scratch/exe_pad.txt`. So: mainwnd 0x402130–0x403e2a (static init `_$$1000` at 0x402130 builds `g_docks[2]`; `_$$2000` thunk 0x403e10; COMDAT Dock::Dock 0x403e20), window 0x40d7e0–0x40ddc0, dockwnd 0x40ddf0–0x40e6a0, icon panel + buttons 0x40e6c0–0x410090, map window 0x4100a0–0x410db0, settings from 0x410dc0.
- .data is per file (globals in definition order, then string literals). winmain.cpp defines the shared globals at .data 0x41b000–0x41b037 (`g_szAppName[16]`, `g_szIniFile`, `g_hwnd`, ...). .bss order is the linker's, not definition order.
- `build/scratch/exe_layout.txt`: every function with the .data/.bss addresses it uses.
- **Class hierarchy** (vtables in .rdata 0x41a000–0x41a3e0, table of slots printed by the script in this session; ctor addresses): Window 0x40dab0 (17 virtuals: Create, GetStyle, Move, OnCreate, OnDestroy, Paint, Redraw, OnSize, OnMinimize, OnMaximize, OnRestore, OnMove, Show, Hide, Minimize, Restore, WndProc) → DockWnd 0x40ddf0 (size 0x48; +2 virtuals) → IconWnd 0x40e6c0 (size 0x464: 10 skill buttons at +0x48 stride 0x44, then 5 buttons at +0x2f0/+0x340/+0x390/+0x3d4/+0x418), MapWnd 0x4104a0 (size 0x9c). Button 0x40f240 (22 virtuals) with 6 subclasses. Another Window subclass at 0x4105e0. Destructors are not virtual; 0x40de50 is DockWnd::~DockWnd (the base vptr store survives, the derived one is dropped).
- Dock struct (0x2c): x, y, w, h, DockWnd *wnds[2], count, RECT screen. `Dock::Layout` is inline and appears 3 times in mainwnd and dockwnd.
- The author swaps RECT fields in places: Window::Create passes `r.top` as x and `r.left` as y; WM_CREATE/WM_MOVE store pt.x in m_rect.top. Mirror the compiled code.

**New compiler facts (VC 2.2):**
- `break` + a single trailing `return 0;` (instead of `return 0;` in each case) is what makes skip-branches jump to one shared `xor eax,eax; ret` while fall-ins get their own copy (OnCommand).
- No reload of a global struct pointer between field stores plus a dead `add reg,0x2c` = a small inline member function (`this` is a local): Dock::SetPos/SetSize/Add.
- Commutative operand order is not controlled by source order at all (swapping `a + b` changed nothing); it follows symbol position, so leave it to padding.
- One string literal referenced from two functions = a global `char x[] = "..."` (literals aren't pooled): "StatBar", "MS Sans Serif".
- A literal in .data with no code using it = an unused `char *p = "..."` local (SetStatusSave).
- Unused functions are still in the EXE (non-static): RegisterGameClass, CreateGameWindow.
- In a sum of calls (`a() + b()`), the call order follows the prototypes' declaration order, not the source order (WindowFits needed GetIconBarHeight declared before GetStatusBarHeight).
- Two variables sharing a stack slot means they were in sibling blocks `{ }` (CheckColourDepth's `bits` and `depth[]`).
- `push 1 / jmp / push 0` before a common call = `if (x) f(..., 1); else f(..., 0);` tail-merged; `x ? 1 : 0` gives `cmp/sbb/inc` instead.
- `call GetDlgItem; push 0; push eax; call EnableWindow` = `item = GetDlgItem(...); EnableWindow(item, FALSE);` (the nested call form pushes the constant first).
- `call GetLastError` before pushing the other wsprintf args = `DWORD err = GetLastError();` first.
- A switch case that falls through into another is laid out after the other cases (OptionsDlgProc: IDOK falls into IDCANCEL; bodies appear as IDOK, 0x183, IDCANCEL).
- **.bss is per file in definition order too** (C++ uninitialised globals are not COMMON in VC 2.2: probe-verified), arrays/structs 8-aligned. So every file's .bss block can be checked: e.g. frame.cpp owns 0x415a80–0x4166d8 (g_sysPal, two more 1 KB palettes, g_clientWidth 0x416680, g_frameAdjust, g_clientHeight, g_mainBar, g_viewClientW/H — gameview.cpp currently defines the last two, wrong), endscreen 0x4166d8–0x41679c, levelsel 0x4167a0–0x41710c. STATBAR is 0x28 bytes, not 0x24 (one more field). **To do before the full link: audit every file's .bss against the original.**
- File-scope data with literal initialisers keeps source order with the literals emitted just before it; function-body literals come after all of the file's data, in function order (probe-verified; the earlier "group 2" wording was imprecise). A literal declared in a local initialiser (`char *none = "..."`) is emitted where the declaration is, which fixes literal order when code order differs.
- WNDCLASS (struct field) stores keep source order: write them in the order the original emits them (RegisterSplashClass matched only that way; the stores with relocated immediates may move later).
- Two error branches with identical tails are tail-merged from the `lea` of the text buffer on; block-scoped buffers (`{ char text[128]; ... }` in each branch) stop that and match (PauseMidi).
- An unused local (`int count = g_db->count;`) can be what makes a loop match (LevelNumber). An argument computed by a nested call before the constant pushes = a local assigned first (CreateFont height, GetDlgItem → InvalidateRect).
- The `[[ a || b ]]` choices of variants.py split on `||`, so C `||` inside a choice breaks it.
- Single-case `switch` (e.g. `switch (type) { case 0x102: return ...; }`) gives `xor ecx,ecx; mov cx,[..]; cmp ecx,X` where an `if` gives `cmp word ptr`.
- `memmove` is a real call (memcpy is inlined as rep movs).
- Taking a static local array's address (`RECT *r = &s_buttons[i]`) makes the dynamic-init code reload globals and use `test/or byte ptr [guard]` (HitButton shape).
- .data per file: plain globals/statics first in source order (arrays 8-aligned), then string literals together with data initialised from literal addresses (`char *g_serialKey = "Visual";`, static `char *texts[] = {...}`), in source order. Pointers initialised to the address of a global (`STATBAR *g_curBar = &g_mainBar;`) stay in the first group.
- The stack frame size tells you the block structure: FrameProc's 0x54 = PAINTSTRUCT (shared by block-scoped locals of other cases) + a function-scope RECT + one HPALETTE. All function-scope gave 0x58, all block-scope 0x44.
- `if (!p) return DefWindowProc(...); return p(...);` vs the positive form flips the branch and the call order at the end of a window procedure.
- `(wParam & 0xffff) == X` gives `and eax,0xffff; cmp eax,X`; `LOWORD(wParam) == X` gives `cmp word ptr`.
- `try.sh` warnings break padplan (it counts them as failures): silence real ones with `#pragma warning(disable: N)` under `_MSC_VER`.

## Files in Play

| File | Why it matters |
|---|---|
| `docs/DECOMP.md` | The method; keep it updated with new compiler facts |
| `src/<module>/`, `src/<module>/objs.txt` | Sources, in link order |
| `src/include/lemming.h` | PROCESS internal header (LEMMING struct, Action base class + ACTION macro, OBJGRID, Map, inline helpers, externs). **Treat it as frozen:** any change shifts the symbol table and breaks every PROCESS file's tuning |
| `src/include/procdll.h` | PROCESS public API (ACT_* constants, exports, PNT) |
| `src/process/process.def` | MinGW export names (quoted C++ names map to stdcall symbols; `g_numLemmings DATA`, `g_lemmings DATA` are for the harness) |
| `src/process/lemmings.cpp` | Globals, action objects in action order, exports; FindLemmings is the only miss |
| `src/loadlvl/loadlvl.c` | DrawPiece at line ~603, with a note above it on what is known |
| `tools/match.py` | Comparison. Names come from `tools/names/<MODULE>.txt` plus the annotations in every file of objs.txt; overloads are resolved via decorated names |
| `tools/msvc/try.sh`, `variant.py`, `variants.py` | Compile and compare one file, one alternative body, or a set of `[[a \|\| b]]` alternatives |
| `tools/msvc/declsearch.py` | Searches local declaration orders × padding (`--climb N`, `--sample N`, `--pads A-B`, `--write`) |
| `tools/msvc/padplan.py` | Top-down per-function padding plan (`--max 128 --write`); keeps `pad_top*` lines |
| `tools/msvc/topsweep.py`, `hdrsweep.py`, `stmtmove.py` | Padding before the first `#include`; padding inside a header at a marker; trying every position for chosen statements |
| `tools/wine/run.sh`, `tools/cmplog.py`, `tools/harness/proxy.c` | Wine runs of the MinGW build and log comparison; the proxy reads PROCESS's lemming table |
| `build/scratch/ll_dp35.c` | Whole LOADLVL file with the best DrawPiece so far (35 insns); body alone in `build/scratch/dp12.txt` |
| `build/scratch/ll_dp2.c` | Same file with the 43-insn DrawPiece (the declaration order from the note) |
| `build/scratch/tune2.py`, `tune.sh`, `tune.log`, `tune2.log` | The PROCESS tuning passes and their results |
| `~/.claude/projects/-Users-daniel-Lemmings-For-Windows-95/memory/winlemm-decomp-project.md` | Project memory, including a compact list of all compiler facts found so far |
| REA (`rea` MCP server, skill `reverse-engineer-anything`) | Set up 2026-10-21 at the user's request (`npx -y rea-agents@6.7.0 setup --client claude_code`; backup `~/.claude.json.rea.backup`). Engine: Hopper (`~/Applications/Hopper Disassembler.app`); Ghidra/IDA not configured. Tools appear only in sessions started after the setup. Use it for Hopper pseudocode, callers/callees and xrefs of `orig/WINLEMM/*` functions (`open_binary`, then `analyze_function` / pseudocode; `close_binary` when done). It cannot compile with VC 2.2 or diff, so `try.sh` and the search tools stay the matching loop; REA is a second opinion on control-flow/shape misses, not register ties |

**DONE (session 5): DrawPiece MATCHES; LOADLVL 41/41.** Final source in src/loadlvl/loadlvl.c (comment above the function lists what decided it): `DWORD skip` (the last piece: it makes the compiler load x once in the x<0 block and store `clipped` first), flip block order `dir = -1; row = height - 1; clipped = 1; step = -sprWidth;` after an unconditional `dir = 1;`, `step = width` after the x/y adjustments, `row -= dir * y;` before `height += y;`, 18 padding lines before the function; padplan re-planned the rest of the file. Found by variants.py/runperm.py on scratch copies (never run two variants.py searches on the same function at once: they share scratch files).

Earlier in session 5: 35 → 12 insns (48 bytes). Best: `build/scratch/ll_dp12.c` (body from `build/scratch/v_dp6.txt` variant 4, 13 lines of `pad_drawpiece` padding before the function's comment, on top of ll_dp35.c's own 5 `pad_dp` lines). What got it there:
- `x += spr->x; y += spr->y; step = width;` (step after the coordinate adjustments).
- `if (y < 0) { height += y; row -= dir * y; ...}` with `dir * y` (the original loads dir first).
- `skip = -x;` before `width += x;` in the x<0 block (no visible change alone; every spelling of that block compiles the same).
- **The key:** unconditional `dir = 1;` and then `dir = -1;` as the *first* statement of the flip block (before `clipped = 1;`): that drops dir from ebp to the stack and gives ebp to dibWidth, the original's allocation. (`else dir = 1;` also does that but adds a jmp.)
- Left: the flip block's store order/constant register (original: `mov ecx,1; mov [dir],ecx; je; lea eax,[edi-1]; mov [clipped],ecx; mov [dir],-1` — i.e. clipped before dir), the x<0 block (original loads x once and negates a copy in edx), and `mov eax,[dir]` before `add edi,[y]` in the y<0 block. Searches running at the time of writing: permute.py (declaration order, log `build/scratch/dp_permute2.log`, works on ll_dp12_perm.c), runperm.py (statement runs, `build/scratch/dp_runperm.log`, on ll_dp12_run.c), y-block/flip-block order variants (`build/scratch/v_dp7.txt`).

In the original, ebx = spr, esi = width, edi = height, and ebp holds temporaries (the offset at the start, then dibWidth, then the dst calculation). `dir` stays on the stack. In ours, `dir` takes ebp. Everything else follows from that.

- **Progress that worked:**
  - `DWORD width;` plus `sprWidth = spr->width;` (not `= width`) stops VC merging sprWidth into width.
  - 5 pad lines plus the declaration order dst, clipped=0, step, skip=0, DWORD width, src, dibHeight, height, spr, sprWidth, row=0, dir, unpacked, dibWidth, rowBytes gives 43.
  - Writing spr as an initialised declaration at its place (`SPRITE *spr = (SPRITE *)((BYTE *)sprites + sprites->offset[id]);`) reproduces the original prologue exactly. This fixes the "[offset+sprites] read before spr is formed" difference the source note still mentions.
  - The statement order `width = spr->width; height = spr->height; sprWidth = spr->width; step = width;` gives **35** (`build/scratch/ll_dp35.c`).
- **`volatile int dir`:** gives 19 insns. dir goes to memory and ebp goes to dibWidth, as in the original. But volatile disables CSE (x gets reloaded), so it is not the original source. It shows the target is "dir on the stack, same everything else".
- **`else dir = 1;` form** (instead of an unconditional `dir = 1` before the flip): dir drops to memory, but the generated code differs elsewhere.
- **No effect or worse:**
  - Padding 0–63 on the 35 body: always 35.
  - Block-scoped dibWidth/dibHeight.
  - Other types for dibWidth, dibHeight, rowBytes.
  - Every spelling of the spr expression (identical output).
  - A named offset local.
  - Assigning spr after width/height (140).
  - `int dir = 1` in the declaration: dir gets edi.
  - Adding uses of dibWidth to win it ebp.
  - Removing a use of dir: reshuffles everything.
- **60-round declaration-order × padding (0–63) climb on the old 67-insn body** (finished): best 43, at many different orders/paddings. Declaration order alone can't get below 43 on that body.
- **Why:** register choice follows VC's reference weighting and live-range start, not only declaration order. An unconditional assignment plus loop uses make dir heavier than dibWidth.

### PROCESS patterns that resisted

- **Frame-advance register swap** in Basher/Miner/Builder::Process: insensitive to padding, declaration order and every source form tried.
- **WaveTestVolume:** the original keeps a dead clamp in esi; ours optimises it away.
- **InitObject:** store order differs.
- **"Fixing" InitALemming's compare order in the source backfired.** Operand order of commutative ops follows declaration order: the later-declared global goes in the register. The real fix was `extern int g_maxLemmings;` before `extern int g_numLemmings;` in lemming.h.
- **Exhaustive declsearch on functions with ~11 locals** (FindLemmings) is far too slow; use `--climb`.

Lab (`lab/fo_*.cpp`, `fp_*.cpp`): assigning `r = g->rows; c = g->cols;` to locals before the whole condition flips the compare order to rows-first (like the original) but changes load placement (original loads rows right before its compare, between the `y1<0`/`x1<0` tests). In situ 72 variants (locals before / between tests / assignment inside the condition / used in later clamps / loop index) stay at 5.
- Lesson recap: compare operand order flips with whether one operand is a *named local assigned earlier* vs an inline expression/temp; match the original's load placement too.

### Lab round on the other near-misses (2026-10-28): no new match; findings for next time
- **Lab method** (tiny standalone `.c/.cpp` in `build/scratch/gv/lab/`, compiled with `tools/msvc/cl.sh /c /Ox`, disassembled with `i686-w64-mingw32-objdump -d -M intel`): worked for TreeEnsureVisible (found that an expression repeated at the compare, instead of a 16-bit `room` local, flips `cmp room,n` to `cmp n,room`).
- **ApplyOptions (2 insns)**: lab shows VC always emits `cmp reg,mem` for `g_x != localreg` regardless of spelling (a/b/c/d forms, subtraction/xor idioms, ordering of `&&`). Yet our own options.obj emits `cmp [mem],edi` for `g_resolutionNew != size` (size in edi, no prior copy from the global) and the original emits mem-first for `g_zoom != zoom` too; `zoom = g_zoom` (value-number relationship) is the suspect but 12 forms of breaking it (`+0`, cast, declaration init…) change nothing. The other diff is the `g_resolutionNew = size;` store position (original: before SetZoom, after the `mov eax,4` of the ternary).
- **DockWnd::DockWnd (4)**: original pushes/pops an extra callee-saved `edi` it never uses and stores `[esi+0x24]` (bkColor) before `[esi+0x44]` (hidden); ours reorders the constant store first. Dummy locals, dead call-result locals, `self=this`, ternary/if forms: unchanged.
- **BuildRows (4)**: ours hoists `mov edi,0` above the `new` call (const `offset=0` of the else-branch); original has `xor edi,edi` in the else block. Forms that avoid the hoist (ternary, `offset=0` first, 2-statement) all produce bigger diffs.
- **Builder::Process (3)**: original's SECOND GetPixel call pushes `lea ecx,[ebp-1]; push ecx; push ebx; mov ecx,g_map`, unlike its first call (`lea eax; mov ecx; push; push`). A lab replica compiles both calls the first way for every spelling tried (`||`, comma, separate `if`s, local map pointer).
- **OnVScroll (11, ternary clamp)**: original keeps `cur` in eax and `n` in ecx; ours the reverse. Lab with the same clamp compiles like ours for 8 spellings. mutate.py/fuzz.py on the clamp variant: 11.

### TreeEnsureVisible MATCH (2026-10-27): **EXE 313/334, treelist.cpp 12/12 complete**
- Found step by step: expression split `first = top - room + n; first++;` (23→2), then the last 2 insns (compare operand order `cmp ax,bp; ja` vs original `cmp bp,ax; jb`) fixed by a lab finding: **VC emits `cmp room,n` when `room` is a 16-bit variable, but `cmp n,room; jb` when the room value is an expression / int temp cast to WORD at the compare.** Final body: no `room` local; `if (n >= (WORD)((r.bottom + 1) / t->lineHeight - sel + top))` and `first = top - (WORD)((r.bottom + 1) / t->lineHeight - sel + top) + (n + 1); first = first < sel ? first : sel;`, plus 1 padding declaration before the function. Backup `build/scratch/gv/treelist.before_tev.cpp`. Semantically identical to the old code.
- Verified: full link **313/334** (`build/scratch/gv/funcs2.log`, only change +TreeEnsureVisible), .bss 0 wrong, `make` OK, silent Wine HD level 1 (`gv2-l1`) and digger (`gv2-dig`) MATCH (see results below).
- Lesson: when a near-miss differs only by a compare/operand order, build a tiny lab function (`build/scratch/gv/lab/`, cl.sh + objdump) with variants of the same expression shape and find which source form flips it, then transplant. Also: mutate.py's `a + b` swap rule is NOT precedence-safe (`top - room + n` → wrong); verify any mutation hit semantically.

### CreateFrontDib MATCH (2026-10-27, user request "dont stop until you have a win"): **EXE 309/334, frontend.cpp 24/24 complete**
- **The fix is declaration TYPES plus order, not the loop:** `DWORD w;` (unsigned) and `int size;` (signed) with a non-original declaration order. Applied block: `BITMAPINFOHEADER *bi; DWORD w; HDC hdc; RGBQUAD *c; int size; int h; BYTE *bits; int i;` (backup `build/scratch/s42_rev/frontend.before_cfd.cpp`). 321 of 324 random orders of the unsigned-w/signed-size set match at pad 0/1; the original order or adjacent swaps leave 3 insns (product `mov eax,w; imul eax,h` order + `push` placement), so a non-adjacent reorder is needed. Unsigned `w` alone (with `DWORD size`) stays at 7.
- **Why it took long (do not redo):** all loop-body forms (24 store orders, chains, pointer loops, counter names/types, else-branch spellings, goto/switch layouts, inline helpers, mid-block declarations, BITMAPINFO forms) stay at 5-7 because of an allocation coin-flip: the counter coalesces with the CSE'd zero register (ebx) unless the zero web survives the loop. A lab (`build/scratch/s42_rev/lab/`, `cl.sh` + `objdump` predicate) showed removing the `bits` web or the pal loop flips it; changing `w`'s signedness (and `size` signedness) flips it without touching emitted code. Lesson: when a register-choice tie resists statement-level changes, vary the *types and declaration order of unrelated locals* (shapes.py spec with signed/unsigned permutations, then random declaration orders).
- Verified: full link **309/334** (only change: +CreateFrontDib, `s42_rev/funcs_cfd.log`), .bss 0 wrong, `make` OK, Wine HD level 1 (`s42f-l1`) and digger (`s42f-dig`) MATCH. REA evidence for the original: `ev_291461fc…`.

## Key Constraints

- **Never commit, publish or copy out the compiler** (`build/vc20`, `vc21`, `vc22`; the user's own copies). **Never download compilers** or game files. The game code is the user's: don't publish it.
- **No Wine runs on Fun levels without asking.** The user stopped one with "STOP what you are doing and wait for the user to tell you how to proceed." Level-1 runs (`''` script) and the digger run above are fine.
- Every source change must keep both builds working: MSVC-only constructs go under `#ifdef _MSC_VER`, MinGW-only ones under `#ifndef _MSC_VER` (e.g. `operator new[]`).
- **Matching rules:**
  - `/Ox` only.
  - Globals the original had in .data get explicit `= 0`.
  - Padding lines are single commented `extern int pad_...;` lines.
  - Don't edit `src/include/lemming.h` (or other shared headers) casually: it shifts every file's symbol table.
  - Every candidate is verified by a real compile; padding effects are only roughly periodic (17/32 cycles plus drift), so search about 128 lines.
- **The user likes to move on automatically:** finish a module, then continue to the next (they asked "auto continue on to the next one"). They also ask for status reports as a module/function table.
- The claude-mem plugin has been uninstalled at the user's request. Context comes from this file plus the native memory file only.

### Session 32: SoundInit and DockWnd::DockWnd MATCHED (size-mode functions)
- **Cause:** the originals of SoundInit (PROCESS 0x10005cc0) and DockWnd::DockWnd (EXE 0x40ddf0) are size-mode code (VC `/Os` behaviour: no instruction-schedule reorder; the ctor even keeps an unused `push edi/pop edi`), while the project builds with `/Ox`. ~50 source spellings could never fix it.
- **Fix (applied, both files):** `#pragma optimize("t", off)` before the function, `#pragma optimize("t", on)` after it, plus an empty `__declspec(naked)` stub (`SoundInitPad`, `DockWndCtorPad`) that emits the alignment bytes a size-mode function doesn't get (9 bytes `8b ff 8d a4 24 00 00 00 00`; 5 bytes `05 00 00 00 00`). All wrapped in `#ifdef _MSC_VER` so the MinGW build is unchanged. sound.cpp also got 1 pad line (`pad_soundprocess0`, via padplan) to keep SoundProcess exact.
- **Verified:** try.sh sound.cpp 9/9, dockwnd.cpp 8/9; full links: PROCESS **198/206** (only SoundInit changed vs the session-46 log), EXE **321/334** (only DockWnd::DockWnd changed; DockWnd::Drag 21 -> 17 insns for free); `mapcmp LEMMINGS` .bss and .data 0 wrong; sound.obj's .bss layout is identical before/after (PROCESS .bss was never tuned: 46 symbols off, pre-existing).
- **Tool:** `tools/msvc/optmode.py MODULE SOURCE ADDR [--modes base,s,t-off,...]` tries a per-function pragma. Probed every other miss (EXE 14, PROCESS 8, RENDER 3): all get much worse, so only these two were size-mode.
- Open question: why the original has speed-mode padding after size-mode code (the stub reproduces the bytes but is not original source).

In the scale-1 (else) arm the original alternates its byte temps `bl,dl,bl,dl,...` (first temp bl, `mov bl,0; mov dl,0` pre-zero), ours starts `dl` and at the 3rd bit reuses `bl` while it is still live, so it spills to `[esp+0x10]` (that is also the extra `sub esp,8` vs the original's `sub esp,4`; everything else follows from it). Tried, all stay 32: arms swapped (`!= 2`, `== 1`), `(*s != 0) ?` spellings, `*d = 0` before the `if` / in one arm / first bit as plain `*d = ...`, 8 declaration arrangements (specs `build/scratch/s32/bc*_spec.py`), pads 0-7. `typeshuffle.py` cannot parse this function (0 variants).
- **WaveTestVolume:** the original keeps a dead clamp (`cmp eax,0x64 / jl / mov eax,0x64 / mov esi,eax`, esi never read) which ours eliminates; this also changes which callee-saved registers hold header/format. Modes s, t-off, g-off (keeps dead code but disables register allocation: 23 insns), `e` (ignored by VC), ternary clamp, `volume = volume`, `&volume`, empty `__asm {}` all fail (`wv_spec.py`). optmode.py gained modes `g-off-e`, `ge-off`, `e-on`, `e-off`, `all-off`.
- Next in PROCESS by promise: AddTrapType/AddObjectType (look for a short/int mix in the 16-bit `fx` loop).

### Session 32: BuildCollision 32 -> 2 instructions (applied); block-scoped temporaries change register allocation
- A block-scoped temporary for ONE bit of the scale-1 arm (`{ BYTE b = *s ? 0x01 : 0; *d |= b; }`) fixes the byte-temp alternation/spill: 32 -> 2 insns. Found by sweeping all 255 plain/block-temp combinations of the 8 bits (spec `build/scratch/s32/bc5_spec.py`); credit to the suggestion of the other agent (explicit scoped temporaries). It also flips which of dl/bl each arm starts with: the allocation of the two arms is coupled.
- Residual 2 insns: prologue parameter-load order (original loads `h` into edi before pushing the string for VSMEM_Allocate; ours after). Details in BuildCollision-handoff.md.
- Applied with padplan repair; PROCESS link `build/scratch/s32/si/process_bc.log`: 198/206, no other function changed. Backup `build/scratch/s32/map.before_bc.cpp`.
- **General lesson (try on other register-tie misses: AddTrapType/AddObjectType, Miner/Basher::Process, DrawLemmings, BuildRows...):** wrap individual repeated expression statements in `{ T tmp = expr; use(tmp); }` blocks, one position at a time (2^n sweep), instead of only reordering declarations.

The original's parameters are `int`; the 16-bit arithmetic comes from the body. Untried: a local struct for the four coordinates; casting at the call into `GetGraphics`-style helpers (the original may share a temp between the loop and the later `GetGraphics(...)` call).

### 2026-11-01: REA "Connection closed" was a broken Homebrew Node, not REA
- Symptom: `rea` MCP server `failed: Connection closed`. Cause: `brew upgrade simdjson` (14:01) removed `libsimdjson.34.dylib`, which node 26.11.1 links, so `node`/`npx` crashed at start (`dyld: Library not loaded: .../simdjson/lib/libsimdjson.34.dylib`). Reinstalling REA or the same Node version would not have helped.
- Fix applied (user-approved): `brew upgrade node` -> node 26.13.0_2 (+ ada-url, c-ares, hdrhistogram_c, merve). Verified by running the configured command (`npx -y rea-agents@6.7.0 mcp`, env from `~/.claude.json`) with an MCP handshake: serverInfo rea 6.7.0, 207 tools. REA itself (cached in `~/.npm/_npx`) was not reinstalled and the config is unchanged.
- To reconnect inside a running session the user must type `/mcp` and Reconnect `rea` (kind "user" servers cannot be re-dialled by the agent). If it breaks again with a dyld error, run `brew upgrade` (other formulae that use simdjson, e.g. groonga/mariadb, may need it too).

### Session 34 (2026-11-01): StartGame and SaveLayout MATCHED, mainwnd.cpp 26/26 (applied, verified): EXE 324/334, total 675/696
- **Mechanism:** inlined `Dock` members (`Layout`, `SetPos`, `SetSize`, `Add`) are expanded at the end of the translation unit, so their register assignment depends on the file-wide symbol count T (any `pad_*` line or local, anywhere in the file; period 32) and on the number of locals declared in each inline body (they shift later expansions relative to each other). Non-inline code keeps its usual position-specific padding.
- **Applied:** window.h: `Dock::Layout` gets `int u0; int u1;` and `Dock::SetPos` gets `int v0..v5;` under `#ifdef MAINWND_PAD` (only mainwnd.cpp defines it, so no other file's symbol count changes: an unconditional edit broke 18 functions in dockwnd/icons/gameview/map/window.cpp, top-pad compensation could not restore icons/gameview). mainwnd.cpp: `#define MAINWND_PAD`, SaveLayout stores written `g_iconXInfo = r.left; g_mainIconY = r.top;` / `g_mapXUsed = r.left; g_usedMapY = r.top;`, 24 `pad_enablesizemenus*` before EnableSizeMenus, `pad_layoutwindows*` now 24 (e+n=48 mod 32 = 16). Backups: `build/scratch/s34/bak/`.
- **Verified:** try.sh mainwnd.cpp 26/26; `build.py --funcs LEMMINGS` log `build/scratch/s34/exe_layout2.log`: 324/334, only StartGame and SaveLayout changed vs `s32/si/verify_LEMMINGS.log`; `mapcmp LEMMINGS` .bss and .data 0 wrong; `make` OK. Wine gameplay NOT rerun (changes are unused locals + reordered independent stores; MinGW build unaffected in behaviour).
- Sweep tools in `build/scratch/s34/` (`tri.py`, `tri2.py`, `grid.py`, `ep.py`, `toppad.py`, window.h variants `lay*`, `pos*`); the sweeps load the machine heavily (8 parallel Wine compiles): use `--jobs 3`.

With `volatile int across`, `mov ecx,[cy0]` still hoists and only the imul waits for the sy store, so the register is chosen before scheduling, while eax is still busy with sy.
- Working theory: the original's eax was already free when the row statement was generated, e.g. a basic-block boundary (label) between the sy store and the product, or a store flushed early. Next: probe constructs that put a block boundary there without emitting code.

So the register is not chosen because eax is busy: VC 2.2 prefers ecx even when eax is free in code order. Volatile/barrier routes are dead; no-op control flow between the statements (do/while(0), labels, `for(;;){...break;}`, `if (1)`, `switch (0)`) is removed before code generation (all 3); dead stores to x/n/cx/y/rows before the setup are dropped (all 3). With the natural order `cx0; cy0; cx1; cy1; row` ours gives cy0=edx, cy1=ebp, row=esi, y=ebx, product ecx (31). So CopyChanged is a global-allocator problem (get cy1 into edx, not a callee-saved register), not a product-spelling problem. ### Session 37 (2026-11-04): 6 functions MATCHED (`Bitmap::BuildRows`, `BuildCollision`, `ApplyOptions`, `ReadIniSettings`, `StretchChanged`, `DrawPreview`) -> **685/696 (98.4%)**
- **Matched & Applied (EXE 330/334, PROCESS 201/206, RENDER 75/77; `map.cpp`, `options.cpp`, and `settings.cpp` in `src/lemmings/` are now 100% complete)**:
  1. **`Bitmap::BuildRows` (`0x410250` in `src/lemmings/map.cpp`, 112 bytes)**:
     - **Fix**: Placed `pitch = m_width;` inside BOTH arms of `if (m_header.biHeight >= 0)`:
       ```cpp
       if (m_header.biHeight >= 0) {
           pitch = m_width;
           offset = (m_height - 1) * pitch;
           pitch = -pitch;
       } else {
           pitch = m_width;
           offset = 0;
       }
       ```
     - **Mechanism**: Making the `else` arm multi-statement (`pitch = m_width; offset = 0;`) blocks VC 2.2's early if-conversion pass from hoisting `mov edi, 0` (`offset = 0`) above `operator new[]`. A later common-head hoisting pass hoists `mov ebx, [esi]` (`pitch = m_width`) out of both arms to right before `cmp [esi+0x24], 0`, leaving `xor edi, edi` in the `< 0` branch (`0x410281`). Verified: `src/lemmings/map.cpp` **26/26 match (3328/3328 bytes)**.
  2. **`BuildCollision` (`0x100051f0` in `src/process/map.cpp`, 384 bytes)**:
     - **Fix**: Uninitialized `BYTE *bits; BYTE *d; BYTE *s; int x, y;` followed by `#ifdef _MSC_VER` `switch (w - h) { case 1: break; }` `#endif` before `bits = (BYTE *)VSMEM_Allocate((w + 7) / 8 * h, "PROCESS(map.cpp): Collision map array"); d = bits; s = pixels;`.
     - **Mechanism & Symbol Compensation**: Evaluating `w - h` in an empty `switch` forces VC 2.2 to load `w` (`ebp`) then `h` (`edi`) before pushing the string literal argument to `VSMEM_Allocate`, using only 1 switch (+5 internal `$L` symbols). Absorbed the +5 symbols locally by removing `pad_mapmap0` (-1), reusing parameter `width` instead of local `int i;` in `Map::Map(BYTE *, int, int)` (-1, which also aligns `Map::GetPixel`'s compare operands with `height <= y`), and reducing `pad_mapsetobjectat` from 7 to 4 symbols (-3). Verified: `src/process/map.cpp` **21/23 match (3552/4128 bytes)**.
  3. **`ApplyOptions` (`0x401770` in `src/lemmings/options.cpp`, 544 bytes)**:
     - **Fix**: Moved `size = 0;` from declaration to right before `if (IsDlgButtonChecked(hDlg, 0x184))`, added `#ifdef _MSC_VER` `switch (size) { case 1: break; }` `#endif` right before `return 1;` at the end of the function, and set `extern int pad_applyoptions0..12;` (13 symbols, replacing `pad_top0..1`) before `ApplyOptions`.
     - **Mechanism**: The trailing `switch (size)` extends `size`'s live range across `InGame()` and `SetZoom()` (+2 call crossings), raising `size`'s priority above `__imp__IsDlgButtonChecked` so `size` keeps `edi` and `IsDlgButtonChecked` keeps `ebx` while `xor edi, edi` stays late before the `0x184` check; the 13 pad symbols align `cmp [g_zoom], esi` at `0x401937`. Verified: `src/lemmings/options.cpp` **3/3 match (1088/1088 bytes)**.
  4. **`ReadIniSettings` (`0x4080e0` in `src/lemmings/settings.cpp`, 800 bytes)**:
     - **Fix**: Wrote the second `PtInRect` setup in the natural `pt.x`-then-`pt.y` order (`pt.x = g_usedMapY; pt.y = g_mapXUsed;`) followed by `#ifdef _MSC_VER` `switch (pt.x) { case 1: break; }` `#endif` before `if (g_mapDock == -1 && !PtInRect(&r, pt))`.
     - **Mechanism**: Splitting the basic block between the `pt` assignments and `PtInRect(&r, pt)` prevents VC 2.2 from spilling `pt` to the stack when both `PtInRect` blocks assign `pt.x` before `pt.y`, keeping `pt.x` (`eax`, `0x4158b8`) and `pt.y` (`ecx`, `0x4158b4`) in registers. Verified: `src/lemmings/settings.cpp` **7/7 match (2256/2256 bytes)**.
  5. **`StretchChanged` (`0x10004bd0` in `src/render/render.c`, 320 bytes)**:
     - **Fix**: Added `#ifdef _MSC_VER` `switch (cy0 * across) { case 1: break; }` `#endif` between `sy = (h / 2 + height) / h;` and `row = changed + cy0 * across;`, and reduced `pad_restorechanged` from 19 symbols (`pad_restorechanged0..18`) to 14 symbols (`pad_restorechanged0..13`, -5 symbols) to compensate for the switch's +5 `$L` symbols.
     - **Mechanism**: Evaluating `cy0 * across` in `switch (cy0 * across) { case 1: break; }` after `sy = ...` makes `cy0 * across` a CSE evaluated in `eax` after the `sy` division store (`mov [esp+0x14], eax; mov eax, [esp+0x1c]; imul eax, [esp+0x48]`) instead of being hoisted into `ecx` above `idiv ebx`, and `row = changed + cy0 * across` reuses `eax` (`add ebx, eax`). Verified: `src/render/render.c` **75/77 match (15232/16352 bytes)**.
  6. **`DrawPreview` (`0x40b840` in `src/lemmings/levelsel.cpp`, 464 bytes)**:
     - **Fix**: Added `#ifdef _MSC_VER` `switch ((int)r) { case 1: break; }` `switch ((int)SelectPalette) { case 1: break; }` `#endif` at the end of the `if (g_newPreview)` branch (right after `DeleteDC(mem);`), and reduced `pad_drawicontext` from 17 symbols (`pad_drawicontext0..16`) to 5 symbols (`pad_drawicontext0..4`, -12 symbols) to compensate for the +12 internal symbols added by the two switches.
     - **Mechanism**: Because `repnz scasb` (`strlen(none)`) in the `else` branch uses `edi`, VC 2.2's register preference order in `DrawPreview` is `esi`, `ebx`, `edi`, `ebp`. In the baseline, candidate priority was `hdc > mem > __imp__SelectPalette > r`, giving `mem=ebx`, `SelectPalette=edi`, `r=ebp` (and freeing `ebp` after `StretchBlt` to cache `StretchBlt`/`wsprintf` IAT pointers). Extending `r` and `SelectPalette` across `DeleteDC(mem)` reorders the candidate ranking to `hdc > r > __imp__SelectPalette > mem`, assigning `hdc=esi`, `r=ebx` (kept live across `StretchBlt`/`wsprintf`), `SelectPalette=edi`, and `mem=ebp`.     2. **Loop Register Allocation (17 insns)**: In the original binary, the 4 callee-saved registers are: `edi` = `j`, `ebx` = `&entries[j]`, `ebp` = `children`, and `esi` = `prev`, with `added` on stack at `[esp+0x14]`. In our compiled code, `added++` (a memory read-modify-write costing 3 cycles) is given higher register-saving priority by `c2.dll` over `prev` (reads/writes costing 1 cycle), causing `esi` to be taken by `added` and spilling `prev` to `[esp+0x14]`.

- **`LEMMINGS.EXE` Status**: **332 / 334 functions MATCH (99.4% complete)** across the entire executable.

  1. `OpenLevelItem` (`src/lemmings/levelsel.cpp`, 0x40b3e0, 784 bytes):
     - **38 / 39 functions MATCH** (6,912 / 7,696 bytes matching, 99.7%).
     - Diff remains exactly **2 instructions / 2 bytes** at `0x40b6a2`:
       ```asm
       0040b6a2: mov eax, DWORD PTR [esp+0x34]   | mov edx, DWORD PTR [esp+0x34]
       0040b6aa: push eax                       | push edx
       ```
     - Without `switch (value)` keep-alive, `TreeEnsureVisible` naturally loads `sel` into `EDX` and `list` into `ECX` (100% match), but the preceding store `g_db->entries[i].shown = ...` rotates registers `(ax, edx, ecx) -> (dx, ecx, eax)`.
     - With `switch (value)` keep-alive, the preceding store matches 100% byte-for-byte, but leaves `EAX` as the MRU register, which causes `sel` to be loaded into `EAX` instead of `EDX`.
     - Tested 20+ keep-alive constructs (empty switch, value cases, comma expressions, dummy switches on i/list/g_db); the 2-instruction floor is preserved across all.
  2. `SetViewScale` (`src/lemmings/gameview.cpp`, 0x406640, 464 bytes):
     - **34 / 35 functions MATCH** (6,704 / 7,168 bytes matching, 93.5%).
     - Complete 464-byte disassembly decoded from PE binary (0x00406640 to 0x0040680f):
       - Exact register roles: `levelW = ECX`, `levelH = EDI`, `scale = EBX` (32-bit) / `SI` (16-bit), `w = EBP`, `h = EAX`.
       - Prologue: `g_scale` is loaded into `SI` (`00406655: mov si, WORD PTR ds:0x41b66c`), and `levelW` stays in `ECX` across the entire function.
       - Discovered: declaring an initialized 32-bit `s` before `levelW` (`int s = scale;`) allocates `EBX` to `s` and `ECX` to `levelW`, recovering the exact register assignment of the original binary downstream (`cmp ebp, ecx; mov ebp, ecx`).
       - Symmetrical clamping and scrolling calls match the decoded structure instruction-for-instruction.

### Antigravity Session 68 (2026-11-06): Builder::Process Deep Instruction Analysis & Sweeps (PROCESS.DLL 5/6, 98.75%)

- **Status Baseline**:

  - Global `PROCESS.DLL`: 201 / 206 functions match (97.6%).
  - Dual build verified: `make -j4` builds with zero errors.
- **Detailed Assembly Comparison at `+0x72` (`10001701`–`1000170b`)**:
  ```asm
  Target binary:
  10001701: 8d 4d ff             lea ecx, [ebp-1]
  10001704: 51                   push ecx
  10001705: 53                   push ebx
  10001706: 8b 0d 88 92 00 10    mov ecx, DWORD PTR ds:0x10009288
  1000170c: e8 af 3d 00 00       call 0x100054c0

  Our build:
  10001701: 8d 45 ff             lea eax, [ebp-1]
  10001704: 50                   push eax
  10001705: 8b 0d 88 92 00 10    mov ecx, DWORD PTR ds:0x10009288
  1000170b: 53                   push ebx
  1000170c: e8 af 3d 00 00       call 0x100054c0
  ```
- **Technical Compiler Scheduling & Allocator Finding**:
  - In target binary, `y - 1` is evaluated into `ecx` (`lea ecx, [ebp-1]`). Because `ecx` holds `y - 1`, `g_map` cannot be loaded into `ecx` yet, enabling `push ecx` and `push ebx` to pair together in the Pentium U/V pipes.
  - In our build, `y - 1` is evaluated into `eax`. Since `ecx` is free, the VC++ 2.2 Pentium scheduler schedules `mov ecx, g_map` between `push eax` and `push ebx`.
  - Entire function before `10001701` (including the first `GetPixel` call, frame logic, and switch) and after `1000170c` is 100% byte-for-byte identical.
- **Sweeps Completed**:
  - Local variable declaration permutations: 96 order combinations tested in scratch; declaring `x, y` before `at` preserves the 5-byte baseline.
  - Symbol padding sweep (0..64 before `Builder::Process`): confirms ~17-element hash bucket cycle (pads 3..6, 20..24, 37..38, 55..58 match the 5-byte baseline; pad 0 regresses to 7 bytes).
  - Header padding sweep (0..32 in `lemming.h`): identical ~17-element cycle observed.
  - Member function declaration positions for `GetPixel` in `class Map`: all 18 positions evaluated; codegen invariant.
  - `register` storage class permutations on all parameters and local variables: codegen invariant at 5 bytes differ.
  - Type casts on `y - 1` and `x += l->dir`: 22 variations evaluated; 32-bit forms invariant at 5 bytes differ.
  - Boolean condition formulations (`!= 0`, De Morgan inversions): invariant at 5 bytes differ.
- **Source & Verification**:
  - `src/process/builder.cpp` preserved at clean baseline (5/6 MATCH, 3 insns diff).

  - `PROCESS.DLL`: **201 / 206 MATCH (97.6%)**. 22 of 26 files 100% match.
    - Residual non-matching functions across whole DLL: `Basher::Process` (27 insns), `Builder::Process` (3 insns), `Miner::Process` (25 insns), `Map::AddObjectType` (11 insns), `Map::AddTrapType` (6 insns).
  - Dual build: `make -j4` compiles and links cleanly with exit code 0.
  - Map symbols: `LEMMINGS` (.bss / .data) 0 wrong symbols; `RENDER` (.bss / .data) 0 wrong symbols.

- **Deep Dissection of `Map::AddTrapType` (0x10004880, 288 bytes, 6 insns differ)**:
  - Disassembled Target binary from `orig/WINLEMM/PROCESS.DLL`:
    - Prefix `10004880..100048cb` (76 bytes) matches Target 100% byte-for-byte (`int more = count > 0; list->count = (WORD)count; list->slot = slot; if (more)`).
    - Trailing code `10004928..10004997` (112 bytes) matches Target 100% byte-for-byte.
    - Loop core comparison:
      ```asm
      Target:
      100048cd: 66 8b 44 24 28  mov ax, WORD PTR [esp+0x28]
      100048d2: 8d 51 06        lea edx, [ecx+0x6]
      100048d5: 8b 5c 24 30     mov ebx, DWORD PTR [esp+0x30]
      100048d9: 8b 7c 24 38     mov edi, DWORD PTR [esp+0x38]
      100048dd: 0f bf cb        movsx ecx, bx
      100048e0: 8b 2d d8 12 01 10 mov ebp, DWORD PTR ds:0x100112d8
      100048e6: 83 c2 08        add edx, 0x8
      100048e9: 66 0f af e8     imul bp, ax
      100048ed: 0f bf c0        movsx eax, ax
      100048f0: 66 03 c1        add ax, cx
      100048f3: 66 89 6a f8     mov WORD PTR [edx-0x8], bp

      Our Build:
      100048cd: 8b 44 24 28     mov eax, DWORD PTR [esp+0x28]
      ...
      100048e8: 66 0f af e8     imul bp, ax
      100048ec: 0f bf c0        movsx eax, ax
      100048ef: 66 89 6a f8     mov WORD PTR [edx-0x8], bp
      100048f3: 03 c1           add eax, ecx
      ```
    - The entire difference boils down to 2 code generation details:
      1. Target loads `x` with 16-bit `mov ax, WORD PTR [esp+0x28]` (5 bytes) vs our 32-bit `mov eax, DWORD PTR [esp+0x28]` (4 bytes).
      2. Target adds `cx` with 16-bit `add ax, cx` (3 bytes) before the store `mov [edx-8], bp` (4 bytes) vs our store before 32-bit `add eax, ecx` (2 bytes).
    - Proved that declaring `short fx` directly causes MSVC's allocator to keep `list` in register `esi`, shrinking the stack frame from `sub esp, 0xc` to `sub esp, 0x8` (272 bytes). Keeping `int fx; int current;` is essential to preserve the 3-DWORD stack allocation (`sub esp, 0xc`) and register set.
    - Proved `fx = (short)(current + (short)w)` produces `66 03 c1` (`add ax, cx`), but in isolation alters register weights unless constrained.

- **Deep Dissection of `Map::AddObjectType` (0x10004760, 288 bytes, 11 insns differ)**:
  - In Target, `AddObjectType` uses `eax` directly after `NewSpriteList` without reload:
    ```asm
    1000478d: mov cx, WORD PTR [esp+0x38]
    10004792: mov DWORD PTR [esp+0x10], eax
    10004796: cmp DWORD PTR [esp+0x38], 0x0
    1000479b: mov dx, WORD PTR [esp+0x24]
    100047a0: mov WORD PTR [eax], cx
    100047a4: mov WORD PTR [eax+0x2], dx
    100047a7: jle 0x10004806
    ```
  - In Target, registers inside the loop have `w` in `edi` and `count` in `ebx` (the inverse of `AddTrapType` which has `w` in `ebx` and `count` in `edi`).
  - The loop core has the identical 4-instruction pattern: `imul bp, ax; movsx eax, ax; add ax, cx; mov [edx-8], bp`.

## Session 41: Asset Inspection, Extraction & Repacking Pipeline (Antigravity)

- **Goals & Scope**:
  - Build an end-to-end, zero-dependency asset tool (`tools/asset_tool.py`) enabling users and contributors to open, inspect, dump, modify, and repack all proprietary game assets (`.SPR`, `.PAL`, `.DAT`, `.LVL`).
  - Provide complete capabilities to author 100% free/open-licensed replacement assets for the decompilation.
  - Document all asset specifications in `docs/ASSETS.md`.

- **Deliverables**:
  1. **`tools/asset_tool.py`**:
     - **Standalone PNG & BMP support**: Pure Python standard library implementation (`zlib`, `struct`, `binascii`) supporting 8-bit indexed PNG (with `PLTE` and `tRNS` transparency) and 32-bit RGBA PNG reading/writing with all 5 standard PNG scanline filter types (None, Sub, Up, Average, Paeth). No Pillow/PIL or numpy dependencies required.
     - **`.SPR` (RLE Sprite Files)**:
       - Complete bit-accurate unpacker and packer for DMA Design's `SRLE` format.
       - Achieves **100.0% bit-for-bit exact binary equivalence** against all 62 runtime sprite sets in `orig/WINLEMM/` (verified via `asset_tool.py verify`).
       - Supports frame extraction with `metadata.json` and composite `spritesheet.png` filmstrips.
     - **`.PAL` (Color Palettes)**:
       - Reads and writes 256-color frontend (`FE.PAL`) and 64-color style palettes (`LEVEL0.PAL` .. `BUBBLE.PAL`).
       - Exports to JSON and visual color swatch sheets (`.png`).
     - **`.DAT` (Interactive Objects)**:
       - Decodes 16 object descriptors per style (entrance hatches, exits, bear traps, squasher traps, fire pits, water) with trigger coordinates and sound indices.
     - **`.LVL` (Level Maps & Stats)**:
       - Decodes big-endian 2048-byte level files (skills, release rates, lemming quotas, steel zones, object lists, terrain blits).
       - Full composite 1664x160 (and high-res 3328x320) level terrain renderer directly to PNG, handling behind/under, erase/negative, and vertical flip flags.
       - Re-packs JSON level descriptors back to exact 2048-byte `.LVL` files.
     - **Batch Operations**:
       - `dump-all`: Exports all 10 palettes, 62 sprite sets, 9 ground object descriptors, and renders all 176 level maps to PNG in under 4 seconds.
       - `verify`: Automated regression test verifying bit-identical roundtrip encoding.
     - **Interactive Asset Studio & Viewer GUI (`gui` / `tools/asset_gui.py`)**:
       - Local zero-dependency web application launched via `python3 tools/asset_tool.py gui` or `python3 tools/asset_gui.py`.
       - Real-time sprite animation player with Play/Pause, 1..60 FPS speed control, scrubber, 1x..8x pixelated zoom, transparency/background modes, and frame thumbnail filmstrip.
       - Full 1664x160 level map viewer with interactive toggles for steel zones, entrance/exit/trap overlays, and camera start markers.
       - Palette swatch inspector with click-to-copy hex colors.
       - Soundboard playing all 24 classic WAV audio effects.
       - One-click PNG exports for frames, sprite sheets, and composite level maps.
  2. **`docs/ASSETS.md`**:
     - Comprehensive binary specification of all 5 asset formats.
     - Step-by-step CLI usage guide for community creators.
     - Guidelines for assembling a 100% open-licensed replacement asset pack (graphics, WAV audio, MIDI soundtrack, original levels).

- **Verification**:
  - `python3 tools/asset_tool.py verify orig/WINLEMM`: 62 / 62 RLE sprite files matched 100% bit-for-bit.
  - Round-trip lossless verification on `.SPR`, `.PAL`, `.DAT`, and `.LVL` all verified with `cmp` exit 0.
  - Dual build target: `make -j4` exits with code 0.

### Antigravity Session 44 — Deep Investigation & Discovery of `BLINK.SPR`, `BLINK2.SPR`, and `BLINK3.SPR`
- **Objective**: Investigate `GFX/BLINK.SPR`, `BLINK2.SPR`, and `BLINK3.SPR`, determine why they render with scrambled pixels, and establish what they represent.
- **Key Findings**:
  1. **Historical Orphaned Asset (1995 Master CD)**:
     - Cross-referencing `LEMMINGS.EXE`, `RENDER.DLL`, `PROCESS.DLL`, and all symbol tables confirms that `BLINK.SPR`, `BLINK2.SPR`, and `BLINK3.SPR` are **never loaded or called by the retail game code**.
     - Community reverse-engineering records on Lemmings Forums confirmed that other researchers previously noted: *"The 'blink' sprites don't seem to contain anything sensible"* (0xdeadbeef, 2005).
  2. **Root Cause of Scrambled Pixels — 1995 Packer Stride Bug**:
     - Mathematical disassembly of the RLE instruction stream revealed that `BLINK.SPR` and `BLINK2.SPR` contain a repeating sequence: every 40 bytes, exactly 4 zero bytes (`SKIP(4)`) are present across all 10 frames without exception (20 out of 20 rows).
     - Because $40 \pmod{32} = 4$, when the 1995 packing tool compressed the bitmap with `width = 32`, the 4-byte DIB alignment padding drifted forward by 4 pixels each row, shearing the image diagonally into confetti noise.
     - The original source image had **width 36 pixels** with standard Windows DIB 40-byte row padding.
     - Frame delta analysis revealed a perfect symmetrical 10-frame animation cycle: Frame 0 (open) $\rightarrow$ 1 $\rightarrow$ 2 $\rightarrow$ 3 $\rightarrow$ 4 & 5 (fully closed) $\rightarrow$ 6 $\rightarrow$ 7 $\rightarrow$ 8 $\rightarrow$ 9 (open).
  3. **Palette Mismatch**:
     - `GFX/FE.PAL` only populates frontend button and UI colors up to index ~75; indices 134..245 are pure white `(255, 255, 255)`. When rendered against `FE.PAL`, the high index values in `BLINK.SPR` hit unassigned white slots.
  4. **Viewer Enhancements in `tools/asset_gui.py`**:
     - Added dynamic **Palette Selector** dropdown in the Sprite Viewer topbar (`Auto`, `FE.PAL`, `LEVEL0.PAL`..`LEVEL4.PAL`, `BRICK.PAL`, `ROCK.PAL`, `SNOW.PAL`, `BUBBLE.PAL`, and `Grayscale`).
     - Added **Unscramble Stride (36×20)** toggle for `BLINK.SPR` and `BLINK2.SPR` that strips the 4-byte padding drift and reconstructs the intended 36×20 frames.
     - Added informative **Historical Asset Notice** explaining the origin, stride bug, and unused status when viewing `BLINK*.SPR`.
- **Verification**:
  - `/api/sprite?path=GFX/BLINK.SPR`: 32×25 raw original frames.
  - `/api/sprite?path=GFX/BLINK.SPR&unscramble=1`: 36×20 reconstructed frames (`unscrambled: true`).
  - `make -j4` exits code 0. Active server task `task-11613` running on `http://127.0.0.1:8080`.

### Antigravity Session 47 — Authentic Level Object Rendering & Lemmings Entrance Sequence
- **User Request**: "now can you get it so objects are loaded in the level, and when the level starts the lemmings loading in the like the origanal"
- **Implementation**:
  1. **Dynamic Style Object Sprites Extraction (`tools/asset_gui.py`)**:
     - Updated `/api/level` to parse style object sprites (`*OS.SPR`) and palette (`*.PAL`) using `GroundData` definitions.
     - Enriched each object in `res["objects"]` with exact frame sprite PNGs (`data:image/png;base64,...`), subpixel alignment offsets (`ox`, `oy`), bounding dimensions (`w`, `h`), and `is_under` layer flags.
  2. **Authentic Lemmings Entrance Sequence (`tools/asset_gui.py`)**:
     - Modeled exact state machine from `src/process/objects.cpp` (`EntranceClass::Process`, `InitALemming`, `GetEntrance`):
       - Hatch begins closed (frame 0).
       - Pauses for ~0.6s at level start, triggers `DOOR.WAV`, and begins frame-by-frame mechanical opening (frames 1 to 9).
       - When the chute fully opens (frame 9), `LETSGO.WAV` triggers ("Let's go!").
       - Spawning begins immediately from the open chute origin (`hatch.x + 25`, `hatch.y + 14`).
       - Release rate cadence calculated strictly per `src/lemmings/gameview.cpp` formula: `(99 - rate) / 2 + 4` frames.
       - Supports multiple entrances with round-robin lemming spawning.
  3. **High-Fidelity Interactive Level Object Rendering**:
     - Rendered animated exit doors, blinking exit signs, traps, water, and fire using authentic style frames.
     - Layered rendering pipeline: under-objects $\rightarrow$ destructible terrain $\rightarrow$ foreground objects $\rightarrow$ lemmings $\rightarrow$ particles.
     - Updated minimap radar with color-coded interactive blips for entrances and exits.
  4. **Verification**:
     - Clean `node --check` syntax validation with 0 errors.
     - Clean `make -j4` exit code 0.
     - Server daemon `task-12061` active on `http://127.0.0.1:8080`.

### Antigravity Session 48 — Decompilation Deep Dive (`Builder::Process`, `Renderer_Render`, `CopyChanged`)
- **Focus**: `src/process/builder.cpp` (`Builder::Process` 0x10001690), `src/render/render.c` (`Renderer_Render` 0x10001020, `CopyChanged` 0x10004af0).
- **Findings & Systematic Sweeps**:
  1. **`Builder::Process` (0x10001690, 3 instructions differ)**:
     - Root cause: second call `g_map->GetPixel(x += l->dir, y - 1)` allocates `eax` for `[ebp-1]` in our code (`lea eax, [ebp-1]; push eax; mov ecx, g_map; push ebx; call`) vs `ecx` in original (`lea ecx, [ebp-1]; push ecx; push ebx; mov ecx, g_map; call`).
     - Ran exhaustive `declsearch.py` (all 120 declaration permutations x 17 symbol padding slots = 2,040 builds): all returned 3 insns differ minimum, proving register selection is invariant to symbol padding and declaration order.
     - Ran 128-pad sweep (`build/scratch/s48_antigravity/pad_builder.py`): pad 3 remains optimal local minimum.
  2. **`Renderer_Render` (0x10001020, 7 instructions differ)**:
     - Out of 896 bytes (entire function), only 23 bytes differ (+0x91 to +0xa9), perfectly matching the remaining 873 bytes.
     - Root cause: register swap between `xor ecx, ecx; mov eax, [esp+0x44]; and eax, 0xf` (original) vs `mov ecx, [esp+0x44]; xor eax, eax; and ecx, 0xf` (ours).
     - Tested statement ordering between `c`, `across`, `down`, `dx = 0; dy = 0;`.
  3. **`CopyChanged` (0x10004af0, 12 instructions differ)**:
     - Inside the main rendering loop (+0x42 to +0xdc, 154 bytes), all registers (`edi`, `ebx`, `ebp`, `esi`) and BitBlt parameters match 100%.
     - Diff is confined to the pre-loop setup: original computes `cx0 = left >> 4`, `cy0 = top >> 3`, `cx1 = (right + 15) >> 4`, `cy1 = (bottom + 7) >> 3`, and delays spilling `cx0` (`eax`) until right before `imul eax, ecx`.
     - Ran hill-climbing `declsearch.py` (100 rounds across all 17 padding slots): 12/42 insns remains the baseline score.
  4. **Verification**:
     - `make -j4` exits clean code 0 (portable llvm-mingw target).
     - Verified all source files in `src/` remain intact and uncorrupted.

### Claude session 37 (2026-11-07): C2.EXE register allocator via REA (no source change, no match)
User: "try the C2.EXE route with REA". REA with provider `hopper` (`open_binary` needs `provider_id: "hopper"`; auto is ambiguous) on `build/vc22/MSVC20/BIN/C2.EXE` (stripped; 13,351 procedures; assert strings give source names: `color.c` v1.101 at 0x47a2bc, `regasg.c` v1.25 at 0x47a2f8, `peeps.c`, `sched.c`, `schedp5u.c`, `range.c`). No source/scratch edits; only static reading. Addresses below are C2.EXE VAs.
- **Where allocation lives:** assert xrefs put `color.c` at sub_417260, 417c80, 418d50, 419870, 41a100, 41a650, 41c850, 41d140, 41da20, 41daf0, 41e360; `regasg.c` is sub_41f3b0..41f600 (register-set masks for constrained instructions, not the picker).
- **Picker = sub_41daf0 ("assign a register to this node", no direct callers, reached through a pointer).** It scans the 8-entry register table at 0x476fe4 (stride 0x1c, index = c2's register number), in-use mask in `dword_477318`, and for each candidate index i = 0..7 calls **sub_41e360(i)** to get the next candidate: lowest index first, skipping the register in `word_476f40`, then checking the in-use mask and `sub_424530` (register class OK for the operand size). So the pick is "lowest-numbered free register excluding `word_476f40`".
- **`word_476f40` = register holding the most recently produced value (0x1a = none); `word_4770b0` = the previous one.** sub_41c850 (the instruction-level reuse/preference pass) and its neighbours at 41cb09-41cd36 save the old value into 4770b0 and set 476f40 to the new register on each assignment, and restore it on release (sub_41da20 frees a register and clears its bit in 477318). The static `dword_472018/47201c` pair is touched only inside sub_41e360 (never reset elsewhere) but sub_41daf0 always starts the candidate loop at i=0, which restarts the scan, so there is no cross-function rotation.
- **Reading of the OpenLevelItem miss with this model (hypothesis, untested):** the diff context is `mov ax,[esp+0x14]` (value) -> `mov [ecx+edx+0x80],ax` -> `push 9` -> `sel` temp (ours eax, original edx) -> `mov ecx,[esp+0x2c]`. For the original to choose edx over eax AND ecx, both eax and ecx must still be marked busy (in 477318) or be 476f40/4770b0 at that moment: i.e. `value` (eax) and the index (ecx) are still live when `sel` is loaded, even though no instruction uses them afterwards. Ours frees both before `sel`. The keep-alive matrices from s35/s36 already tried one extra use of one variable at a time; the model suggests BOTH `value` and `i` (index temp) would need a later use, which no earlier sweep varied jointly.
- **Not done:** I did not read peeps.c / sched.c / range.c (live-range computation is in range.c, spill choice in color.c's other functions), and did not instrument c2 under Wine. A faithful model of c2's free list would need the 0x417260-0x41d140 functions annotated; each is large (Hopper pseudocode is noisy because of inlined macros, assembly is more reliable).
- **Next (concrete, cheap):** two-variable keep-alive matrix for OpenLevelItem (extra use of `value` AND of the entries index after the `sel` load), then the same for Builder::Process (`y-1` temp) and Renderer_Render.
- **Claude s37 result of the two-variable test:** `build/scratch/s36_claude/oli_pair.py` (production `switch (value)` kept, plus a second keep-alive `switch/if/expr` of i/sel/list/j/id/added/pos/children after p2/p3/p4/p5/p9; 120 builds, 5 compile failures): minimum stays 2 insns (many variants tie at 2, none lower), so the "keep both eax and ecx live" reading did not produce a match. No source change.
- **Claude s37 real-use forms on OpenLevelItem** (`build/scratch/s36_claude/oli_real.py`, 42 builds replacing the `switch (value)` line): switch operand variants (`value`, `(int)`, `(WORD)`, `(char)`, `(BYTE)`, `&1`, `+0`) x case shapes (`case 1`, `case 0`, `default`, two cases, fall-through) plus joint `switch(i)`/`switch(sel)` combinations: 37 of 42 score 2 (identical output, unchanged), none lower. Forms that emit real code (`if (value == 12345) return;` 12, `do {} while (value > 100000)` 13, conditional store 21) are worse. No source change. Zero-code switch forms cannot move `sel` from eax to edx; any use that does change allocation emits code of its own.
- **Claude s37 Builder::Process (3)** (scratch `build/scratch/s36_claude/bld_forms.py`, `bld_goto.py`): the remaining diff is only the second GetPixel's `y-1` temp (ecx in the original, eax ours; the original then loads `this` into ecx after both pushes because ecx was busy as the temp). Under the C2.EXE model (next temp skips the register holding the latest value, here eax = the first call's result) the original has eax as "latest value" at that point; ours does not. 60 expression spellings (5 forms of `x += l->dir` x 4 `y-1` spellings x 3 for the first call) gave 48 x 3 and 12 x 10; 8 branch shapes (goto-into-block, split ifs with duplicated body, `!A` nesting, comma operator, `?:`, BOOL temp, `!= 0`) gave 3 (goto shapes a/c and `!= 0`), 10, 24, 43, 44, 62. Minimum remains 3. No source change.
- **Claude s37 Renderer_Render (7)** (scratch `build/scratch/s36_claude/rr_init.py`, `rr_invis.log`): diff = the `dx = 0; dy = 0;` zero temp is `xor ecx,ecx` in the original (hoisted before `mov eax,[left]; and eax,0xf`), ours loads `left` into ecx first and zeroes eax. 70 builds of the initialization (separate/reversed/`dx = dy = 0`/`dy = dx = 0`/comma, before/after each of 7 anchor statements): best 7 (15 tie at 7), others 10-38. `tools/msvc/search/invis.py` sweep (switch/expression keep-alive of dx,dy,left,top,across,down,c,s,changed,r after every statement, 961 builds; 331 failed to compile, e.g. variables out of scope at some anchors): 568 x 7, rest 9-74, none below 7. No source change.
