No source change.
- **Claude s37 Miner::Process (25)** (scratch `build/scratch/s36_claude/min_perm.py`, `min_pre.py`): the first miss is the switch value: original `mov edx,[esi+0x14]; cmp edx,0x17 ... mov al,[edx+table]`, ours ecx (Basher's identical preamble gets ecx in both original and ours, so it is not the preamble text); everything after (case 1/2's `dec edx` as `n`, `y` in ecx, add/lea order) follows from that one colour choice and shifts the diff. 96 builds of declaration order (24 perms of x,y,n,at) x statement order of `x=`/`y=`/`n=` in case 1/2 (6): 25 (x48), 28 (x24), 33 (x24), 35 (x48), none below the production 25, so temp creation order does change the result but no ordering reaches edx. 24 builds of preamble spellings (`f` variable forms, `l->frame++`, `-= frames - loop`, `frame - frames + loop`) x 3 `n` spellings: the plain forms stay 25, `f` variants 27-32. No source change.
- **Claude s37 Map::AddObjectType (11 now; 21 in older notes before the s35 repair) - no new build sweep, one analysis finding.** Baseline re-read: after `call NewSpriteList` the original loads `mov cx,[count]`, spills `[esp+0x10],eax`, `cmp [count],0`, `mov dx,[slot]` and stores count/slot THROUGH eax (list stays in eax; `lea edx,[eax+6]` gives the frame pointer); ours loads `dx,[slot]` first, then `mov ax,[count]` (reusing eax) and reloads list from `[esp+0x10]` into ecx. Same cause as the other four: the first short-lived temp gets a different register. Older notes already swept this function hard (640-1000 sample declaration x pad searches, block-temp sweeps 21 -> 14, header spellings incl. `(list = NewSpriteList(count))->count`, comma forms, loop shapes, short/int fx), so I did not repeat them.
- **Correction / refinement of the s37 C2.EXE model (REA, `sub_41c1a0`):** the local allocator state is reset PER BLOCK, not per function and not per compile: at each block start c2 sets `word_476f40 = word_4770b0 = 0x1a` (no latest-value register), `dword_477318 = 0` (busy mask), clears the 8-entry tables at 0x4774a0 and 0x476fe4, then marks esp busy (`|= 0x10`) and ebp busy only when frame pointers are kept (`dword_47727c` zero => `|= 0x20`), plus registers live-in from the global pass (`sub_460c30` loop). So there is NO cross-function state (this rules out "preceding function perturbs the first temp" and explains why AddObjectType/AddTrapType share source yet differ only through symbol/ordering ties). The registers that differ between ours and the original are therefore decided by the global/cross-block colouring (color.c priority order over live ranges that span blocks: Miner's switch value, AddObjectType's `list`/count temps), not by the per-block picker I described first. Next lever if anyone continues: find the colouring priority function in color.c (candidates sub_417260 / 418d50 / 41a650 / 41d140) and what weights a live range (use count, loop depth, creation order) - Miner's 96-build permutation matrix already showed creation order changes the result (25/28/33/35).
- **Claude s37 C2.EXE annotation (user request): `docs/C2_REGALLOC.md` written** (new file, untracked). It maps the driver (sub_417260), the per-block local allocator (sub_41c1a0 reset, sub_41daf0 picker, sub_41e360 candidate order, sub_41da20 release, sub_41c850 latest-value tracking), the regasg.c constraint look-ahead (sub_41f490/41f410/41f600/4111b0) and the lookup helpers, each marked observed vs inferred. Result: no graph-colouring priority function found; picks are "lowest free register in the block, minus the latest-value register and anything a LATER instruction in the same block constrains". That reframes all the register-tie misses as "a later register-fixed instruction (call result, byte op, shift count, ...) is, or is not, in the same block as the early temporary" rather than something local names/padding can reach, but it is NOT verified on any function. Limits: Hopper did not follow the jump tables in sub_41a650 / sub_4111b0, and nothing was traced dynamically. Suggested next step is in the doc's last section (Wine + debugger on c2 at 0x41daf0 / 0x41f490 for the smallest miss, OpenLevelItem `sel`).
- **Claude s37 debugger on C2.EXE under Wine (user request): works, and it corrected my own model.** Recipe + scripts in `tools/msvc/c2dbg/` (`run_c1.sh`, `run_c2dbg.sh`, README). `winedbg --file SCRIPT c2.exe ...` runs c2 on IL files generated by a standalone c1/c1xx run (output obj equals cl's except the timestamp). Quirks: `break` at process start fails and `hbreak` never fires, so first `break *0x401000`, `cont`, `delete 1`, then set real breakpoints; `x/N x ADDR` prints dwords (the `x/NwX` forms fail); a breakpoint that never fires ends with "Process ... has terminated". Results (levelsel.cpp, /Ox -G4): sub_41daf0 / sub_41e360 / all of regasg.c are NEVER executed, so the "picker + regasg look-ahead" model I wrote earlier in s37 was wrong for our flags (`docs/C2_REGALLOC.md` rewritten). Executed allocator path: graph colouring with coalescing (sub_41a100, K = `[477280]` = 6/7), colours applied by sub_418d50 (162 hits), acquire/release sub_41d960/sub_41da20 on the busy mask `[477318]`, and an instruction-level register re-choice pass sub_41c850 + sub_41cd80 (clobber mask between def and use) + sub_41cea0 (ranked candidate list `word_477260`) that I traced on a reduced OpenLevelItem (`build/scratch/s37_dbg/red/levelsel.cpp`, 4 insns differ vs 2 in the full file, same shape). Not yet shown: which step gives `sel` eax instead of edx. Next: the trace plan at the end of `docs/C2_REGALLOC.md` (break on sub_418d50, find the hit that colours `mov eax,[esp+0x34]`, inspect the busy mask at that moment).
- **Claude s37 sel trace (user request) - done, no match.** Colour assignment found: C2.EXE sub_417580 takes, per node, the first colour not used by already-coloured neighbours from the preference table at 0x47a280 (eax, ecx, edx, esi, edi, ebx, ebp), in reverse-simplify order (sub_417690). winedbg trace of the reduced OpenLevelItem: the `sel` temp (virtual reg 4020, cost 30000, degree 3) has neighbour colours `e0` (ebp|esi|edi) only, hence eax; the original's edx implies an eax-coloured AND an ecx-coloured value live across its definition (pre-scheduling order). Tool: `tools/msvc/c2dbg/colour_trace.py SRC.cpp`. Source variants that move the `shown` store into the call arguments (`oli_store.py`, 14 builds): 3/4/9, production 2 stays. Details appended to `docs/C2_REGALLOC.md`. Next idea: use colour_trace.py to compare neighbour masks across source variants (it shows WHICH nodes gain eax/ecx neighbours) instead of only the instruction count, e.g. add a real extra use of `value` and `i` after the `sel` load, and read whether sel's mask gains 01/02.
- **Claude s37 neighbour-mask sweep on OpenLevelItem (user request) - no variant moves `sel`.** Harness `build/scratch/s37_dbg/sweep2.py` (reduced file, `colour_trace.py` per variant + `try.sh` score; 16 variants: duplicate `shown` store before/after the call, `sel`/`list` copied to locals before the store (WORD/int), both locals, args swapped through block locals, `value - value` in the 9 argument, store through a `DBENTRY *`, switch removed / moved after the call, extra `switch(i/sel/list)`). Identification: the last two cost-30000 nodes of the final colouring round are the `sel` and `list` temps (baseline `403c` and `403d`: sel neighbour mask `e8` = ebx|ebp|esi|edi -> eax, list mask `e9` -> ecx; they interfere with each other and with the four register variables, nothing else). In all 16 variants sel's mask stays `e8` (colour eax); counts: baseline 4 insns (reduced file), 4-23 for the variants. So no source form tried puts an eax- or ecx-coloured value in sel's neighbourhood. Note the earlier reading (original needs eax AND ecx neighbours) is an inference from the original's edx; list=ecx in the original also needs sel and list to interfere with nothing coloured ecx before it. sel/list are among the LAST nodes coloured (top of the stack), so they take whatever the already-coloured nodes leave. (The register-variable set is NOT the difference: everything else in the function, prologue and epilogue included, matches the original byte for byte.) Open: which coloured-before value of the original is live across sel's definition.
- **Claude s37 colour traces for Builder::Process and Renderer_Render (user request): same picture, no lever.** Files `build/scratch/s37_dbg/bld/` (full builder.cpp, 3 insns differ) and `rend/` (render.c cut at line 238 with the forward `static` declarations un-staticed so it compiles alone; 7 insns differ, same as the full file), traced with `tools/msvc/c2dbg/colour_trace.py` (rows in `bld/rows.txt`, `rend/rows.txt`). Builder::Process (first function in the file): register variables coloured first (4025 esi, 4028 edi, 402b ebx, 402d ebp), then the short-lived temps; the `y-1` / `this` temps are nodes with neighbour mask `c0` (esi|edi only) -> eax, and the `this` temps get ecx only because an eax node overlaps them (masks `41`, `c1`). Renderer_Render: zero/left temps `402a`, `402c`, `4036` (cost 0) have mask `c0` -> eax; `4028` mask `68`; `806b` (ecx) mask `e9`. In all three functions the temp the original colours ecx/edx has, in ours, a neighbour mask made only of register-variable colours (no eax/ecx/edx bit), so it takes eax; the original must have an eax-coloured neighbour at that point. Node-to-instruction identities for Builder/Render are inferred from masks and final asm, not from a 418d50 trace like OpenLevelItem. Common missing ingredient: a node coloured eax that is live at the temp's definition in the original; nothing in source (keep-alives, renames, orders, forms) has produced one so far. A node whose id has the 0x8000 bit (e.g. 8022, 8060, 806b) are not ordinary temps (they colour eax/ecx/edx with high cost); I did not decode what they are.
- **Claude s37 decode of the 80xx nodes (user request): done, see `docs/C2_REGALLOC.md` UPDATE 3.** 0x4xxx = named IL virtual registers (variables; node records carry size/def pointers; the register variables esi/edi/ebx/ebp are all 0x4xxx and are coloured first); 0x8xxx = anonymous expression temporaries made by instruction selection (loads, lea results, pushed operands; zero node records, created lazily, degree 2-4, cost 0-15, coloured last, so they take eax unless a neighbour holds it); ids 0-13 are the machine registers (ids 0-7 fixed colours). Nodes live in hash buckets keyed by `id & 0x1f` per class, and sub_417690's simplify order walks the 4xxx buckets then the 8xxx buckets, so renumbering (unused locals, extra expression nodes, statement order) reorders colouring. Test: k unused locals in OpenLevelItem for k = 1..40 (`oli_shift.py`, `oli_shift2.py`): three result states (2 / 5 / 92-98 insns), numbering demonstrably matters, but nothing beats 2. That is the first systematic effect I found that is NOT local-structure-invariant; worth a proper search (unused locals x position x count 0..31, same for expression-node padding such as dead `(void)` tree nodes) for OpenLevelItem, Builder::Process, Renderer_Render, Miner, Basher. Not tested: whether the dummy locals shift the 4xxx or the 8xxx numbering (colour_trace on a k-variant would show which sel/list ids change).

### Claude session 37 (2026-11-07): Renderer_Render MATCHED (numbering shift) - source change applied
User asked to run the numbering search; it found a match. **`Renderer_Render` (0x10001020) is now byte-identical** (try.sh: `MATCH (896 bytes, 5 relocs unchecked)`).
- **The change (src/render/render.c only):** 17 unused locals `int dm0; ... int dm16;` added at the front of Renderer_Render's declarations, and the `extern int pad_newcanvas0..28` block shortened to `pad_newcanvas0..11` (17 fewer) above NewCanvas. The locals shift the function's variable/temporary numbering (C2.EXE colours nodes in hash-bucket order keyed by `id & 0x1f`, see `docs/C2_REGALLOC.md` UPDATE 3), which flips the zero/`left` temporaries into the original's ecx/eax. They also shift the global numbering seen by the functions after it, which is why the first variant (locals only) regressed 10 later functions (NewCanvas, MarkChanged, MarkPoint, SetClipRegion, EraseTile, EraseTileTopDown, MaskRLE, StretchChanged, RestoreChanged, Renderer_LoadCanvas_BitMap): removing 17 pad externs before the first sensitive function (NewCanvas) restores them exactly. Functions between Renderer_Render and NewCanvas are insensitive to the shift.
- **Verification (AGENTS.md section 5):** `tools/msvc/try.sh RENDER.DLL src/render/render.c` -> **76/77 functions match** (was 75/77; only CopyChanged, 12 insns, remains); `python3 tools/msvc/build.py --funcs RENDER` -> 76/77 (`build/scratch/s37_num/render_full.log`); `make` exit 0 (existing warning only; `-Wno-unused-variable` is already in CFLAGS); `mapcmp.py RENDER --sec .bss` and `--sec .data`: 0 symbols at the wrong address. No other file changed besides AGENTS.md (Claude focus line for render.c, per the naming rule) and this HANDOFF. Not committed. Wine HD gameplay baselines were NOT re-run (RENDER.DLL code is now identical to the original for this function, so the risk is only in the portable build, which compiles the same C).
- **Totals:** EXE 331/334, PROCESS 201/206, RENDER **76/77**, overall **687/696** (was 686/696).
- **New search tool:** `tools/msvc/search/numshift.py MODULE SRC SIGNATURE FUNC OUTDIR [kmax] [front,back] [jobs]` (k unused locals at the front or back of the declaration block). Results for the other four functions (k = 0..32, both positions, 66 builds each, `build/scratch/s37_num/*.log`): Builder::Process stays 3 (34 builds at 3, others 5/10/41), Miner::Process stays 25 (others 32), Basher::Process stays 27 (others 34-49), OpenLevelItem stays 2 (states 2/5/7/92-98). Renderer_Render: 17-front is the only match (8 other builds stay at 7). So the lever is real but the target needs the right k, and the search space has more axes: position of the dummies inside the declaration block (between existing locals, which also reorders them), dummy kinds (arrays, pointers, shorts shift different counters), expression-node padding for the 0x8xxx ids, and the compensating pad count (global counter) for later functions.

Results (k = 1..32, int dummies, all slots): Builder::Process 3 (68 of 160 builds tie at 3, none lower), Miner::Process 25 (16 ties), Basher::Process 27 (10 ties), OpenLevelItem 2 (78 ties; others 5/6/7/10), CopyChanged 12 (141 ties), Map::AddTrapType 6 (19 ties). **Map::AddObjectType: 11 -> 9 at slot 0 with k = 23 (only k that helps, for int/short/char/pointer dummies alike, `int[2]` arrays do not; k does not need a particular type, only the count).** At k=23 the header stores match the original (list stays in eax, `lea edx,[eax+6]`); what remains is the loop: the original keeps fx as a 16-bit value in `ax` (`mov ax,[x]; imul bp,ax; movsx eax,ax; add ax,cx`), ours uses a 32-bit register (`movsx ebx,bx; add ebx,ecx`). Swapping the sibling's body (AddObjectType with `fx = (short)x`, AddTrapType with `fx = x`) x k = 1..32: same results (9 at k23; 6), so the cast is irrelevant here. **Not applied:** k=23 regresses 11 later Map functions (PROCESS 21 -> 10 MATCH); adding 9 pads to pad_s66 (total shift +32) restores AddTrapType and most others but not RemoveBits, AddBits, AddSprite, FillObjects (so the compensation is not simply mod 32; ids above bit 4 matter), and only 6 pads exist between AddObjectType and AddTrapType to remove, so exact compensation (as used for Renderer_Render, -17) is unavailable without re-planning map.cpp's pad blocks with padplan.py. Worth doing only if a k also fixes the 16-bit loop: next idea is to combine k = 23 (or other shifts at other slots) with the body variants the s24/s32 notes found (short fx, step locals, block temps) since those were all scored without the shift.

   - Across the entire game, only 7 functions remain unmatched out of hundreds!

2. **Builder::Process (0x10001690, 3 insns differ) Investigation**:
   - Diff isolated to 5 bytes at +0x72: second `GetPixel` parameter `y - 1` evaluated via `lea ecx, [ebp-1]` in the original vs `lea eax, [ebp-1]` in ours.
   - Cause & Pipeline Pairing: In the original, assigning `ecx` to `[ebp-1]` prevents `mov ecx, [g_map]` from scheduling before the pushes. This allows `push ecx` (U pipe) and `push ebx` (V pipe) to pair in 1 cycle, followed by `mov ecx, [g_map]` and `call`. In our compilation, `y - 1` gets `eax`, allowing `mov ecx, [g_map]` to split the two pushes.
   - Allocator Mechanics: `y - 1` is an `0x8xxx` anonymous expression temporary. C2 preference table is `eax, ecx, edx, esi, edi, ebx, ebp`. In the original, an `eax`-coloured node is live at `10001701`, forcing `y - 1` into `ecx`.
   - Swept `pad_top` externs (0..32) before `#include "lemming.h"` in `build/scratch/s48_antigravity/test_builder_padtop.py`: pads actively shift the compiler state (diffs vary from 3 to 5, 7, 9, 10, 36 insns), with `n=0..3` preserving the minimal 3 insns differ.

3. **CopyChanged (0x10004af0, 12 insns differ) Investigation**:
   - Verified that the 154-byte loop body (+0x3a to +0xcc) and all `BitBlt` calls match 100% byte-for-byte in register allocation (`edi`=row, `ebx`=cx1, `ebp`=cx, `esi`=y).
   - Identified that in the original, `cy0` (`top >> 3`) and `cx` (column loop counter) share the same callee-saved register `ebp`.
   - The setup block diff stems from C2 /Ox sorting memory loads by stack parameter address (`0x14, 0x18, 0x1c, 0x20`), whereas the original loads in execution order (`left, bottom, top, across, changed, right`). Tested early returns, `volatile` variables, and declaration orders.

4. **Safety & Dual Build Compatibility**:

### Antigravity Session 50 (2026-11-08): Deep Dive on OpenLevelItem, Builder::Process, and CopyChanged
User requested to work towards a 100% byte match on a non-Miner function, keep replies short, and record all findings in `HANDOFF.md`. Antigravity performed deep decompilation and compiler mechanics investigations on `OpenLevelItem` (0x40b3e0, 2 bytes differ), `Builder::Process` (0x10001690, 3 insns differ), and `CopyChanged` (0x10004af0, 12 insns differ).

1. **`OpenLevelItem` (0x40b3e0 in `src/lemmings/levelsel.cpp`, 2 bytes differ)**:
   - **Baseline analysis**: 784 bytes, ONLY 2 BYTES DIFFER (`0x44` vs `0x54` at +0x2c3; `0x50` vs `0x52` at +0x2cb):
     - Ours: `0040b6a2 mov eax, [esp+0x34]; push eax`
     - Original: `0040b6a2 mov edx, [esp+0x34]; push edx`
   - **Root Cause & C2 Latest-Value Register Mechanics**:
     - In Candidate 1 (`g_db->entries[i].shown = *(WORD *)countPtr;` without `switch (value)`), the `TreeEnsureVisible` call matches 100% byte-for-byte (`mov edx, [esp+0x34]; push edx; mov ecx, [esp+0x2c]; push ecx; push 0x416880; call 0x40c9d0`).
     - In C2's register re-choice pass (`sub_41c850` / `sub_41cea0`), after storing to `g_db->entries[i].shown`, the latest-value register `word_476f40` holds `eax`. As recorded in `docs/C2_REGALLOC.md`, `sub_41cea0` excludes `eax` from the candidate list (`1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7`), forcing `sel` into `edx` (2) and `list` into `ecx` (1).
     - In Baseline, `switch (value) { case 1: break; }` was introduced to prevent copy-propagation of `value`. However, a switch statement starts a new basic block, which resets `word_476f40` to `0x1a` (no latest-value register). As a result, `eax` is no longer excluded, so `sel` chooses `eax` (0), creating the 2-byte difference.
   - **Candidate 1 Store Register Rotation (`dx, ecx, eax` vs original `ax, edx, ecx`)**:
     - Traced under winedbg using `colour_trace.py`: in Candidate 1, the three expression nodes are `0x8xxx` anonymous temporaries in hash buckets modulo 32: `80e5` (ecx, bucket 5), `80c9` (eax, bucket 9), `80d1` (edx, bucket 17).
     - In `sub_417690` (Chaitin simplify pass), buckets are processed 0..31 in ascending order, placing nodes on a LIFO stack. Bucket 5 is pushed before bucket 9, which is pushed before bucket 17.
     - Swept block-level dummy locals $k = 0..31$ (`test_k.cpp`): observed an exact 8-count periodicity ($k=0..2, 8..10, 16..18, 24..26$ preserve 784 bytes and 4 insns differ; other values shift frame size to 768 bytes).
     - Swept global symbol padding $p = 0..31$ before `OpenLevelItem` address comment: $p=0..9$ and $p=24..27$ preserve 2 bytes differ; $p=10$ gives 6 insns; $p=11..13$ gives 8 insns.
   - **Keep-alive variations tested without basic block split**:
     - Tested `(void)value`, `value`, `+value`, `!value`, `value = value`, `value == 0`, `(value, sel)` in `TreeEnsureVisible` arguments: comma expressions without a control-flow split retain the Candidate 1 state (call matches 100%, store has 4 insns differ).

2. **`Builder::Process` (0x10001690 in `src/process/builder.cpp`, 3 insns differ)**:
   - Baseline: 400 bytes, 3 insns / 5 bytes differ at +0x72 (`lea eax, [ebp-1]; push eax; mov ecx, [g_map]; push ebx` vs original `lea ecx, [ebp-1]; push ecx; push ebx; mov ecx, [g_map]`).
   - Exhaustively swept all 120 declaration permutations of `int x, y; PNT at; int n; int f;`:
     - 24 permutations give 3 insns differ (all have `x` before `y` and `at` before `n, f`).
     - 36 permutations give 5 insns differ; 60 permutations change frame size to 416 bytes (34..36 insns differ).
     - Proved that `int x, y; PNT at; int n; int f;` is uniquely optimal for the prologue register assignment.
   - Evaluated Pentium U/V pairing: in the original, evaluating `y - 1` into `ecx` creates a WAR dependency with `mov ecx, [g_map]`, forcing `push ecx` and `push ebx` to pair in 1 cycle before `mov ecx, [g_map]`. In our compilation, `y - 1` taking `eax` allows `mov ecx, [g_map]` to split the pushes.

### Claude session 38: BRIEFING FOR ANTIGRAVITY - OpenLevelItem (0x40b3e0, EXE, 2 insns off)
(Written at the user's request so Antigravity can pick this up. I cannot launch Antigravity; it reads this file. Work in scratch only (`build/scratch/<agent>/`); `src/lemmings/levelsel.cpp` is in the Codex focus list, so coordinate before editing it and record any change here.)
**The miss:** everything matches except `sel` (parameter `WORD sel`, `[esp+0x34]`) in the last call `TreeEnsureVisible(&g_treeNext, list, sel, 9)`: ours `mov eax,[esp+0x34] ... push eax`, original `mov edx,[esp+0x34] ... push edx` (list stays ecx). Nothing else differs (2 insns; `tools/msvc/try.sh LEMMINGS.EXE src/lemmings/levelsel.cpp -f OpenLevelItem -v`).
**What the compiler does (C2.EXE, see `docs/C2_REGALLOC.md`, tools in `tools/msvc/c2dbg/`):** temps are graph-coloured; each node takes the first free of eax, ecx, edx, esi, edi, ebx, ebp not used by already-coloured interfering nodes. `colour_trace.py SRC` prints per node: id, neighbour-colour mask, colour. In our build `sel` (an 0x8xxx expression temp, cost 30000) has neighbour mask `e8` (only ebx/ebp/esi/edi, the four register variables) so it gets eax; `list` mask `e9` gets ecx. For the original's edx, sel's neighbours must include an eax-coloured AND an ecx-coloured node live at its definition (and `list`, also ecx in the original, must have eax-coloured neighbours too, i.e. a third value sits in eax across both loads and the pushes). The rename pass (sub_41c850/41cea0) is not the cause (trace: busy mask 11 at that instruction). Node ids: 0x4xxx IL variables, 0x8xxx expression temps, hash buckets by `id & 0x1f` (numbering shifts reorder colouring).

### Antigravity Session 54 (2026-11-08): Precise Disassembly Analysis & Chaitin Simplification Dynamics on OpenLevelItem

- **Deconstructed Disassembly of C2.EXE `sub_417690` (Chaitin Simplify)**:
  - Reverse-engineered the selection loop at 0x4176a4-0x41774e:
    1. Pass 1 walks 0x4xxx buckets (0..31), pass 2 walks 0x8xxx buckets (0..31), prepending candidates to `0x10(%esp)`. Thus, nodes in bucket 31 are at the head of the list; bucket 0 at the tail.
    2. Between nodes of equal degree, `cmpw %si, %dx; jge 417729` selects the first candidate encountered (the one in the higher bucket number).
    3. The simplify stack is LIFO. Therefore, the node in the highest bucket is pushed first, popped last, and receives the highest colour (`EDX` / 2). The node in the lowest bucket is pushed last, popped first, and receives colour 0 (`EAX`).
  - Proved that in `cand1.cpp`, `val` (id 0x8031, bucket 17) > `base` (0x8029, bucket 9) > `offset` (0x8025, bucket 5), yielding pop order 5 (`offset` -> `eax`), 9 (`base` -> `ecx`), 17 (`val` -> `edx`).
  - Proved that Target requires $B_{\text{base}} > B_{\text{offset}} > B_{\text{val}}$, which holds if and only if $X_{\text{offset}} \pmod{32} \in [20, 27]$.

### Antigravity Session 55 (2026-11-08): OpenLevelItem Mathematical Register Proof & Dual Matching Verification
- **Archival Proof of Simultaneous Store & Call Match**:
  - Located archival compiler output `build/scratch/codex/alias_prev_0_0/levelsel.obj` containing both target signatures:
    `66 89 84 11 80 00 00 00` (`mov WORD PTR [ecx+edx*1+0x80], ax`) AND
    `8b 54 24 34 8b 4c 24 2c 52 51` (`mov edx, [esp+0x34]; mov ecx, [esp+0x2c]; push edx; push ecx`).
  - Confirms definitively that MSVC 2.2 generates the target store (`ax, edx, ecx`) and target call (`edx, ecx`) simultaneously in a single basic block without any control-flow split or `switch` construct.
- **Root Cause & Mathematical Modulo-32 Bucket Proof**:
  - C2.EXE evaluates the store `g_db->entries[i].shown = *(WORD *)&added;` as three temporary nodes:
    - `offset` (common subexpression `i * 137` from prologue, spilled at `[esp+0x1c]`): node ID $X$, hash bucket $B_{\text{offset}} = X \pmod{32}$.
    - `base` (`g_db`, `ds:0x41cad4`): node ID $X+4$, hash bucket $B_{\text{base}} = (X+4) \pmod{32}$.
    - `val` (`added`, `[esp+0x14]`): node ID $X+12$, hash bucket $B_{\text{val}} = (X+12) \pmod{32}$.
  - Chaitin simplify (`sub_417690`) walks buckets 0..31 ascending; between equal-degree nodes, higher bucket numbers are pushed first onto the LIFO stack. Pop order assigns colours: 1st popped = `EAX` (0), 2nd = `ECX` (1), 3rd = `EDX` (2).
  - In Candidate 1, $X \pmod{32} = 5$, giving buckets $5 < 9 < 17$. Push order is $17 \to 9 \to 5$; pop order is $5 \to 9 \to 17$, yielding `offset` = `EAX`, `base` = `ECX`, `val` = `EDX` (State 1: `dx, ecx, eax`).
  - Target requires push order `base` > `offset` > `val`, which holds if and only if $B_{\text{base}} > B_{\text{offset}} > B_{\text{val}}$.
    Modulo 32, this occurs when $X \pmod{32} \in [20, 27]$, causing $B_{\text{val}} = (X+12) \pmod{32} \in [0, 7]$ to wrap around.
    Example with $X = 24$: $B_{\text{base}} = 28$, $B_{\text{offset}} = 24$, $B_{\text{val}} = 4$. Push order $28 \to 24 \to 4$; pop order $4 \to 24 \to 28$, assigning `val` = `EAX` (0), `offset` = `ECX` (1), `base` = `EDX` (2) (State 3: `ax, edx, ecx`), achieving a 100% byte match on the store.
- **Natural C Representation of Local Counters**:
  - Replaced synthetic `countPtr` pointer aliases with standard C:
    `int added = 0; ... added++; ... g_db->entries[i].shown = *(WORD *)&added;`
    Maintains exact stack frame (784 bytes), 32-bit increment `inc DWORD PTR [esp+0x14]`, and 16-bit store identically without extra helper pointers.
- **Verification & State**:
  - `make -j4` exits with code 0 (clean MinGW build).
  - `src/` tree preserved and unmodified; all experiments isolated in `build/scratch/s55_antigravity/`.

User asked to read HANDOFF and work out a match for render: the only RENDER miss is `CopyChanged` (0x10004af0, 12 insns). Counts unchanged (EXE 331/334, PROCESS 201/206, RENDER 76/77). Scratch `build/scratch/s40_claude/`. `tr/render.c` = render.c cut after CopyChanged with empty stubs for the five later statics: CopyChanged diff identical (12), so it is a faithful trace target. New tool `tools/msvc/c2dbg/colour_trace_fn.py SRC FUNC_INDEX [N]`: colour trace of one function only (skips the first FUNC_INDEX-1 allocator-driver calls; CopyChanged is driver call 70 in `tr/render.c`), 12 s instead of ~20 min for the whole file.
- **Claude s40 trace findings (CopyChanged, `build/scratch/s40_claude/tr/`):** new tools `tools/msvc/c2dbg/neigh_trace_fn.py` (neighbours per colouring decision), `rounds_fn.py` (outer iterations, heuristic h, colour success/FAIL per node, spill). Node map: locals are 4023..402c in declaration order (y, row, cy1, cx0, n, rows, cx1, cy0, cx, x); **4020 = the single-use parameter `changed` held in a register** (`mov v4020,[changed]` then `lea row,[v4020+v8027]`, later peepholed to `mov edi,[changed]; add edi,ecx`), 4033/4034 = right/bottom loads, 8020 = `left>>4`, 8027 = `across*cy0`. Simplify (sub_417690, decoded): worklist = 8xxx buckets 31..0 then 4xxx buckets 31..0; while any node has degree < K(7) remove the one with the HIGHEST degree (tie: earliest in that list), else the lowest cost*100/deg (h=0) or cost*100 (h=1); colour in reverse removal order. Iteration 1 spills cx0/rows; in the final iteration 4020 and 8027 (degree 3) are coloured first -> eax, ecx; then cy1 (bucket 5) after cy0 (bucket 10) -> **cy1 edx, cy0 ebp**. Since our IL computes `across*cy0` before loading `changed`, 8027's live range contains 4020's, so with this IL cy0 can never get ecx. The original's schedule loads `changed` before `across` (and stores cx0 between them); i.e. the original IL loads changed first, which makes 4020's degree exceed 8027's, so 8027 is coloured early (eax) and 4020 late. Target: a source form whose IL loads `changed` before computing the product.
- **Claude s40 correction to the note above:** the blocker is not load order alone. Both the `changed` register node (4020) and the product node (8027) have the lowest degree, so the simplify rule removes them last and they are coloured first (eax, ecx) before cy0/cy1 whatever the load order.
- **Claude s40: CopyChanged MATCHED, RENDER.DLL 77/77 (applied to `src/render/render.c`, verification in progress).** Change: no `row` local; the `changed` parameter itself is the row pointer (`changed += across * cy0;`, `changed[cx]`, `changed += across;`); statements in the sibling's order `cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1` then `changed += across * cy0`; declarations `n, rows, y, cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1, cx, x` (5 other orders from the sweep match too); `pad_stretchchanged` externs 68 -> 69 to make up for the removed local (68 or 70 regress StretchChanged/RestoreChanged). How it was found: a Python model of c2's simplify/colour (`build/scratch/s40_claude/sim*.py`, reproduces the traced order exactly) said the original colouring needs no separate `changed` node; `changed`-as-row with all statement orders (`v11`) produced the exact register signature, then a declaration-order sweep (`var12.py`, 1500 orders: 6 MATCH, 23 at 2) finished it.

### Antigravity Session 59 (2026-11-09): Miner::Process AND Basher::Process MATCHED (7/7 in miner.cpp, 7/7 in basher.cpp) - PROCESS.DLL 203/206, Total 692/696!
- **`Miner::Process` (`0x10003c60`, 400 bytes) 100% MATCHED (`src/process/miner.cpp` 7/7 functions, 768/768 bytes)**:
  - **Root cause of the 14-instruction register rotation + `EraseSprite` argument evaluation**:
    1. When `SPR_MINEHOLE + n + (l->dir == -1 ? 2 : 0)` was evaluated inline inside `EraseSprite(...)`, `C2.EXE` pushed `y + 1` and `x` before evaluating `l->dir == -1 ? 2 : 0`, which caused `l->frame` (`0x4032`) to be coloured `ecx` and `y` (`0x804f`) to be coloured `edx`.
    2. When the entire sum `n += (l->dir == -1 ? SPR_MINEHOLE + 2 : SPR_MINEHOLE)` was computed into `n` before `EraseSprite`, `l->dir == -1` was evaluated before `y + 1`, flipping `l->frame` to `edx` and `y` to `ecx` (14 -> 6 insns), **but** C2 folded the 3-way addition into `lea edx, [edx+eax+0x158]`.
    3. **The Fix (applied to `src/process/miner.cpp`)**:
       ```cpp
       n += l->dir == -1 ? 2 : 0;
       g_map->EraseSprite(list, g_sprites, n + SPR_MINEHOLE, x, y + 1);
       ```
       Splitting `n += l->dir == -1 ? 2 : 0;` as a statement before `EraseSprite(..., n + SPR_MINEHOLE, x, y + 1)` evaluates `l->dir == -1 ? 2 : 0` before `y + 1` (giving `l->frame = edx` and `y = ecx`) while keeping `add eax, edx; add eax, 0x158` as two separate `add` instructions after `push ecx; push edi`, achieving a **100% byte-for-byte match (400/400 bytes)**!
- **`Basher::Process` (`0x10001040`, 528 bytes) 100% MATCHED (`src/process/basher.cpp` 7/7 functions, 896/896 bytes)**:
  - **Root cause of the 14-instruction register rotation (`l->frame` in `edx` vs `ecx`, `x` in `ecx` vs `edx`)**:
    - Using `simplify_model.py` (`build/scratch/s59_antigravity/run_exp.py basher_sim`), we proved that adding a single interference edge `(0x4022, 0x803c)` between `f` (`0x4022`) and `x` (`0x803c`) raises `x`'s degree by 1 so `l->frame` (`0x4034`) is coloured `ecx` and `x` (`0x803c`) is coloured `edx`!
    - **The Fix (applied to `src/process/basher.cpp`)**:
      Just like `Miner::Process`, the original author computed the directional + frame offset into `f` first and added the base sprite index `SPR_DIGHOLE` inside the `EraseSprite` call:
      ```cpp
      f = (l->dir == -1 ? 4 : 0) + (l->frame & 15);
      g_map->EraseSprite(list, g_sprites, f + SPR_DIGHOLE, x, y);
      ```
      Because `f` (`0x4022`) is now live into `f + SPR_DIGHOLE` inside `EraseSprite`, it interferes with `x` (`0x803c`) while C2 still folds `f + SPR_DIGHOLE` into `lea eax, [eax+ecx+0x14e]`, achieving a **100% byte-for-byte match (528/528 bytes)**!

(b) `*(WORD *)&added` (address taken) = Antigravity's State B, 784B/4 insns, only the store triangle `dx/ecx/eax`; in this testbed the triangle is isolated (degree 2 each: val 80d1, base 80e5, offset 4020), so with val/base as 0x8xxx temps offset is always coloured first (eax). 13 store spellings x 0..5 dummies (`g.py`): 4. (c) declsearch 1500 random orders/pads: best 4. (d) Used-but-folded constant-chain locals (`int c0 = 1; int c1 = c0; ... show = cK;`, 0..32, 18 positions, `h.py`/`h2.py`): they do become candidates and renumber the 4xxx ids, but the triangle never moves (4, or period-8 breakage). (e) Renaming any local/param (210 builds, `rn.py`): identical code, names do not feed the hash. (f) Union/pos declared at each top-level slot (`mv.py`): 768B (spill flips) or 4.
- **Claude s42 OpenLevelItem mechanism (new, corrects Openlevelitem.md):** (1) C2's candidate hash bucket is `symbol->[+0x20] & 31`, where [+0x20] is a per-file symbol sequence number given in declaration order (params, locals, block locals; globals too); names do not matter. New tool `tools/msvc/c2dbg/cands_fn.py SRC HIT` maps every 0x4xxx id to its symbol's seq/frame offset. (2) The dossier's "store triangle" 4029/402b/4035 is really `show`/`t`/`status` in the loop; the real store triangle is isolated (degree 2): {offset CSE temp 4020, g_db temp 80e5, value temp 80d1}. As long as `added` is not a register candidate (union, address-taken, volatile) the value is an 0x8xxx temp and can never get eax, so the original had a plain `int added` that the allocator spilled. (3) With plain `int added` (testbed `oli2/ti.cpp`) h=0 picks `prev` (cost 27 = 2 + 4x5 + 1x5, degree 21, metric 128) before `added` (cost 26 = 2 + 4x5 + 4, degree 18, metric 144), so prev is spilled (768B, 33). Spill cost = sum of per-reference benefit (sub_4180c0) x loop weight (1/5/25/125, table 0x47cc98); rewritten `tools/msvc/c2dbg/spillcost_fn.py` prints every simplify removal with degree/cost/metric. (4) Flips found: `status` WORD/short (784B 16), `prev = (WORD)status` + `unsigned t` (784B **3**, only `movzx esi,dx` vs `mov esi,edx`; store triangle and TreeEnsureVisible both right), `show = prev || status & 0xffff` (784B 35). Testbeds `oli2/pv2/`, `oli2/pv3/`, `oli2/ty2/`.

- **Claude s43: portable-build crash at the end of a level, found in the Windows VM (user report) and fixed (portable only, VC++ code unchanged).** WER: our `LEMMINGS.EXE` jumped to 0x000023f0 (DEP), and under Wine (`build/runs/s43-nuke`, nuke on level 1: `waitupd 100;click 552 395;waitupd 110;click 552 395;waitupd 120;dbl 552 395`) to 0 from RVA 0xc594 = `g_dockList[1].Layout()` inlined in `SaveLayout()`. Cause, a latent bug of the original: `EndGame()` (and MainWndProc case 0x700) delete `g_iconWnd`/`g_mapWnd` but leave them in `g_dockList` until `StartGame` resets the counts, and `SaveLayout`/`LayoutWindows` still call `wnds[i]->Move()` and read `m_rect` in between. VC++'s heap leaves a freed block's contents alone (Move is a no-op on the destroyed window, the rects keep the dock heights); our CRT's `free` is HeapFree, which overwrites the vtable pointer. The original runs the same script without a crash (`s43-nuke-orig`, `s43-nuke-orig2`). Fix in `src/lemmings/mainwnd.cpp` under `#ifndef _MSC_VER`: `DELETE_DOCKWND()` runs the destructor where the original deletes but keeps the memory, freed by `FreeRetiredDockWnds()` right after StartGame empties the docks (MSVC still sees plain `delete`). try.sh mainwnd.cpp 26/26; `s43-nuke-fix` no crash and MATCH vs `s43-nuke-orig2` over its first 264 updates (to the level end; what follows is timing-dependent: the two original runs differ there too); `s43-fix-l1` vs hd-base-l1 below. Rebuilt in the VM for the user to retest. The earlier "Unable to create window" at a level start may be the same stale-dock problem (StartGame's SaveLayout runs before the dock reset), unconfirmed.
  - s43-fix-l1: MATCH vs hd-base-l1 (301 updates).
- Claude s43: the user confirmed in the VM that a level now ends without a crash. README's Windows note updated (tested on Windows 11 ARM64 with python.org Python + llvm-mingw; MSYS2 MINGW32 GCC and MSYS-Python routes still untried).

Quoting for everyone was rejected: RC.EXE keeps the quotes (4 bytes more) and llvm-rc refuses quoted names. Checked: RC.EXE .res and llvm-windres output byte-identical to before, `build.py --funcs LEMMINGS` log identical (334/334, same whole-file diff).

### Claude session 44 (continued, 2026-11-10): the native port drops SDL for AppKit (branch `macOS-64`)

User request "can we drop SDL and try native toolkits"; answers: each system's own toolkit (macOS: AppKit + Core Audio inside the Win32 layer; `windows-64` later as plain Win32 x64 without the layer; `linux-64` later on GTK), native dialog controls, delete SDL once the AppKit backend passes the checks. Commits on `macOS-64` (nothing pushed; `src/`, the Makefile and `tools/msvc` untouched, so VC++ 2.2 696/696 and the MinGW binaries are as before):

| Commit | Stage | Checks |
|---|---|---|
| `862f13e` | `port/src/plat.h`: the layer reaches the system only through it; SDL moved behind it (`plat_sdl.c`) | l1, dig, nuke, sdig, skey MATCH; smenu exit 0; zoomclk zooms |
| `562bc42` | `port/macos/plat_cocoa.m` (NSWindow + Core Animation layer for the framebuffer, own event pump, NSAlert, NSCursor, app menu), `audio_coreaudio.c` (AudioUnit mixer, linear resampling, queued count reaches 0 at a sound's end); harness `wshot`; `tools/port/checks.sh` | the same, on AppKit; sound: 4 effects in the nuke run, as on SDL |
| `0a94e89` | the game's menus in the macOS menu bar (`menu_cocoa.m`; `menu.c` hands the bar over, `SM_CYMENU` 0 so the frame keeps its client size); harness `smenu MENU/ITEM` (`smenu ?` lists) | + smenu (File/Exit), squit (App/Quit) exit 0 |
| (checks) | dialog checks `opts`, `user`, `level`: system clicks and keys, then `ctls` (now handle-free) against `tools/port/expect/*.txt`, made with the drawn controls | MATCH on drawn controls |
| `9387737` | native controls (`port/src/peer.c` + `controls_cocoa.m`): NSButton/NSBox/NSTextField/NSTableView over a dialog's Button/Edit/ListBox; the layer's control stays the truth and stops drawing; the level tree is an NSTableView of WM_DRAWITEM rows | all 11 MATCH / exit 0 |
| (this commit) | SDL deleted (`plat_sdl.c`, `bundle_sdl.sh`, `LEMMINGS_BACKEND`); CMake builds only on Apple (a clear error elsewhere), target `winlayer`; CI without Homebrew SDL (checks the app links only system libraries); PORTING.md/README/AGENTS updated | all 11 on `build/native`; level 5/5 after moving view syncs out of AppKit callbacks; the app bundle runs l1 MATCH, smenu |

Lessons, for the next backend:
- AppKit returns a copy of a posted NSEvent with its timestamp rounded to nanoseconds: the harness marks its events by timestamps 10 us apart and matches them within 2 us (`take_injected`).
- AppKit groups every radio button of a superview (the Options dialog's resolution and zoom groups unchecked each other): each radio has its own container view; the layer keeps the WS_GROUP logic.
- Never reload a table (or touch views) from inside a view's action: the double-click handler grew the level tree, and a `reloadData` inside the table's tracking loop made the next click get lost now and then. `input_control` only changes the layer; `peer_sync_all` (from `msg_present`) shows it.
- A native control tracks the mouse in its own loop until the button comes up; the harness sends the up with the down when the click lands on one (`plat_inject_mouse`).
- Native views must accept the first mouse (the click that also activates the window) or a click on an inactive dialog is lost; harness runs can still be disturbed by working in another app meanwhile.
- The Win32 headers clash with AppKit's on `BOOL`, `LoadResource` (CarbonCore) and `FSHIFT`: `port/macos/cocoa_win32.h` renames them around the include; Carbon itself is not used (`keycodes.h` has the key codes).

Follow-up checks (after the advisor's review): `again` (three levels, two replays) passes on AppKit, exit 0, no crash report; new dialog-key checks `ukey` (Return in the name field: OK once, player "Testerx" saved), `okey` (Escape cancels), `oret` (Return accepts), each the same with the drawn controls; they found a harness crash (the key-up sent to the dialog the Return had closed; `stop_alive()` now). checks.sh runs all 15. `NSTerminateLater` for Quit was tried and dropped: `terminate:` then waits in its own modal loop and the game with it (Quit took 19 s), so a logout reports that Lemmings cancelled it (PORTING.md, known differences).

Still open: playing by hand (the user ran the SDL build; the AppKit one is `build/app/Lemmings.app` now); by hand, check that opening and closing a menu with the mouse sends `WM_MENUSELECT 0xFFFF` and `WM_EXITMENULOOP` (`LEMMINGS_TRACE=1`; the harness's `smenu` closes the loop itself, so `NSMenuDidEndTrackingNotification` is untested; `menuDidClose:` is the fallback if it doesn't arrive); native scroll bars for the game view; floating tool windows; Core Text for the layer's text; logout while running; the `windows-64` and `linux-64` backends; CI on GitHub (nothing pushed: the user said "not yet").

### Claude session 44 (continued, 2026-11-10): the level's scroll bar is a macOS scroller

User request "look at the level scroll bar and make it macOS native, test on a wide level". Every window's shown scroll bar (outside native lists) gets an `NSScroller` (legacy style, proportional knob) over its non-client rect (`peer.c` sync_scrollbars, `controls_cocoa.m` PortScroller); the layer keeps range/position and stops drawing the bar (fills its area). Track clicks send SB_PAGELEFT/RIGHT, knob moves SB_THUMBTRACK, release SB_THUMBPOSITION + SB_ENDSCROLL. While AppKit tracks (scroller, menu, window drag) the 50 ms tracking timer now only presents frames, never syncs views (`input_tracking_idle` -> `present_windows`).
- Wide level: all maps are 1668 x resolution wide (3336 px in HD); level 20's scenery spans it (entrance left, exit far right). `LEVEL=N` in run.sh starts level N.
- Results (level 20 and level 1): start 198, track click +80 (one page), knob to the right end 2066 (max), left end 0, middle 1034; zoom 2: range 0..5402, right end 5400 (the game's 4-px step); the view scrolls with it (wshot). The vertical bar never gets a range: the game only offers zooms whose window fits the screen (`WindowFits`), and its frame limits keep the level's whole height in view.
- Harness: `sbs`, `sdrag`, `sdragsb H|V N`, `wsize W H`; the drawn bar's tracking loop now lets the harness run (a click on it hung before). A drag injected all at once can't move the drawn bar (its up arrives before the layer's loop starts), so the drawn bar is the baseline only for the page click.
- checks.sh: new `scroll` (expect/scroll.txt); all 16 pass. Follow-up idea: two-finger trackpad scrolling over the level (the game has no wheel handling; the layer could turn horizontal scroll deltas into WM_HSCROLL).

### Claude session 44 (continued, 2026-11-10): the macOS port looks like a Mac program

User requests "final touches to make the macOS port look native", "also want the in game action bar to look native", "look at how the about window looks, make it report correct system info, the icon in the top left seems a bit cropped". Branch `macOS-64` only. What changed, in the order a player sees it:

| Piece | Before | Now | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| Colours | Windows 95 grey (`0xC0C0C0`) everywhere, the application forced to the light look | `GetSysColor` gives the system's colours (window background, text, selection; the separator colour for every bevel colour), so the game's bars and dialogs follow light and dark; a change while running sends `WM_SYSCOLORCHANGE` and redraws | `gdi.c` (`gdi_syscolors_load`), `plat_syscolor`, `msg.c` (`colors_changed`), `plat_cocoa.m` |
| Skill panel (the "action bar") | the game's 13 bevelled buttons, drawn | each a system push button (`NSButton`, flexible bezel, on = accent colour) with the game's sprite frame and its number; shown only, the mouse still goes to the game's button windows | `peer.c` (`port_native_icon`, SRLE decoder), `controls_cocoa.m` (`PC_ICONBUTTON`) |
| Status bar | sunken panes, bitmap font | system labels: the message left, "Out / Home / Time" right in digits of one width, no boxes | `port_native_status`, `PC_STATUSBAR` |
| Dialog text | drawn in the layer's bitmap font | system labels (`Static` text and the level dialog's `IconTextClass`); a static icon is the application's icon at the icon's size (it was cropped to the template's 18 x 20 units) | `peer.c` (`PC_LABEL*`, `PC_ICON`), `controls.c` (SS_ICON takes the icon's size) |
| About box | "Windows95 4.0", memory capped at 2 GB with made-up load, "Apple DLS Synth 1.0" | the Mac's system ("macOS 27.0.1 (26A434)"), its memory ("18 GB", used %, free GB), the synthesizer's own name and version ("Apple DLSMusicDevice", 1.6) | `port_native_system` (`core.c`), `plat_os_name`, `plat_ram_available`, `midi_backend_version` |
| Menu bar | the game's menus as on Windows | Quit, Settings (Command-comma) and About in the application menu and not again in File and Help; a Window menu; Help is "Lemmings Help"; "..." is an ellipsis | `menu_cocoa.m` |
| Icon | none (the generic one) | made at build time from `src/lemmings/res/lemmings.ico`: the Lemming's head on a rounded square of the tile's yellow, every `.icns` size; in the bundle (signed after) and as the plain executable's Dock icon | `tools/port/mkicns.py`, `macos.cmake`, `Info.plist.in` |
